**Cape Town**[^cite_note-15] is the [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature) [capital](181337.md) of [South Africa](17416221.md). It is the country's oldest city and the seat of the [Parliament of South Africa](376182.md).[^cite_note-16] Cape Town is the capital and largest city of the [Western Cape](313646.md)[^cite_note-17], and the country's [second-most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_South_Africa), after [Johannesburg](58893.md). The city is part of the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) [metropolitan municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)).

The city is known for [its harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town), its natural setting in the [Cape Floristic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region), and for landmarks such as [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) and [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point). Located on the shore of [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay), the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl) area of Cape Town, which contains its [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) (CBD), is [the oldest urban area in the Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Cape_Town), with a significant cultural heritage. The metropolitan area has a long coastline on the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md), which includes a northern section in the West Beach region, as well as the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) area in the south.

The [Table Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_National_Park) is within the city boundaries and there are several other nature reserves and marine-protected areas within and adjacent to the city. These include [Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirstenbosch_National_Botanical_Garden), which contains 5 of South Africa's 6 [biomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biomes), and displays many plants native to the Cape region.

A popular global [tourist destination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Tourism), Cape Town has been named the best city in the world, and the world's best city for travellers numerous times, including by *[The New York Times](30680.md)* in 2014,[^cite_note-18] *[Time Out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Out_\(magazine\))* in 2025,[^cite_note-High_five!_Cape_Town_named_Time_Out's_Best_City_in_the_World-19] the World Travel Awards in 2026,[^cite_note-Cape_Town_earns_double_honours_at_travel_awards-20] and *[The Telegraph](23797577.md)* for the past 8 years (2017 through 2025).[^cite_note-Cape_Town_crowned_world's_top_city_for_travel_–_again-21] Cape Town has South Africa's highest household incomes,[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22] lowest rate of unemployment,[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Deviates_from_National_Labour_Trends-23] highest level of infrastructure investment,[^cite_note-2025_to_2026_Budget_-_Annexure_A-24] strongest service delivery performance,[^cite_note-Cape_Town_ranked_first_in_latest_Good_Governance_Africa_index-25] largest tourism appeal,[^cite_note-Cape_Town_crowned_world's_top_city_for_travel_–_again-21] and most robust real estate market.[^cite_note-CT-26]

## History

Main articles: [History of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Cape_Town) and [Timeline of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Cape_Town)

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon ["Khoisan group renames the cape"](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h1stIhiV5L0)— 2012 news report by [Eyewitness News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyewitness_News_\(South_Africa\)) on the official Khoekhoe naming of Cape Town as "ǁHuiǃgaeb" and how it is pronounced |

### Early period



Portuguese explorer [Bartolomeu Dias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeu_Dias) planting a [Padrão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Padr%C3%A3o) at [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point), 1488

The earliest known remnants of human occupation in the region were found at Peers Cave in [Fish Hoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish_Hoek) in the late 1920s. Subject to much debate, earlier estimations as to an upper [Pleistocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleistocene) origin have since been revised. At their most generous and conservative estimates, D. D. Stynder et al. [radiocarbon date](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiocarbon_date) SAM-AP 4692 to the mid-[Holocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocene) at 5448 and 5136 BCE (or 7457 and 7145 cal BP) respectively.[^cite_note-27]

[Bartolomeu Dias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartolomeu_Dias), the first European to reach the area, arrived in 1488 and named it "Cape of Storms" (*Cabo das Tormentas*). It was later renamed by [John II of Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_II_of_Portugal) as "[Cape of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_of_Good_Hope)" (*Cabo da Boa Esperança*) because of the great optimism engendered by the opening of a sea route to the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent) and [East Indies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Indies). In 1497, Portuguese explorer [Vasco da Gama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasco_da_Gama) recorded a sighting of the Cape of Good Hope.

In 1510, at the [Battle of Salt River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Salt_River), the Portuguese admiral [Francisco de Almeida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_de_Almeida) and sixty-four of his men were killed and his party was defeated[^cite_note-28] by the "Goringhaiqua" (in Dutch approximate spelling) using cattle that were specially trained to respond to whistles and shouts.[^cite_note-29]

The Gorinaiqua were one of the [Khoikhoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khoekhoe) clans who inhabited the area. In the late 16th century French, Danish, Dutch, and English, but mainly Portuguese, ships regularly continued to stop over in Table Bay en route to the Indies. They traded tobacco, copper, and iron with the [Khoikhoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khoikhoi_language) clans of the region in exchange for fresh meat and other essential traveling provisions.

### Dutch period

Dutch period



[Jan van Riebeeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_van_Riebeeck) and Dutch colonists arriving in [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay) in 1652



A diorama of Cape Town as it would have appeared in 1800 at the end of Dutch rule by the VOC

See also: [Dutch Cape Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Cape_Colony)

In 1652, [Jan van Riebeeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_van_Riebeeck) and other employees of the [United East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company) ([Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language): *Verenigde Oost-indische Compagnie*, VOC) were sent to the Cape Colony to establish a way-station for ships travelling to the [Dutch East Indies](23476997.md), and the [Fort de Goede Hoop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_de_Goede_Hoop) (later replaced by the [Castle of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castle_of_Good_Hope)).

The settlement grew slowly during this period, as it was hard to find adequate labour. This labour shortage prompted the local authorities to import enslaved people from [Indonesia](14579.md) and [Madagascar](18964.md). Many of these people are ancestors of modern-day [Cape Coloured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Coloureds) and [Cape Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Malays) communities.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31]

Under Van Riebeeck and his successors, as VOC commanders and later governors at the Cape, a wide range of agricultural plants were introduced to the Cape. Some of these, including grapes, cereals, ground nuts, potatoes, apples and citrus, had a large and lasting influence on the societies and economies of the region.[^cite_note-32]

### British period

See also: [Cape Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Colony)



[Adderley Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adderley_Street) in 1897 was an important commercial hub in Cape Town at a time when the city was the most important centre of economic activity in the Southern Africa region.

With the [Dutch Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Republic) being transformed into [Revolutionary France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_French_Republic)'s vassal [Batavian Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batavian_Republic), Great Britain moved to take control of Dutch colonies, including the colonial possessions of the VOC.

Britain [captured Cape Town in 1795](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_the_Cape_Colony), but it was returned to the Dutch by treaty in 1803. British forces occupied the Cape again in 1806 following the [Battle of Blaauwberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Blaauwberg) when the Batavian Republic allied with Britain's rival, France, during the [Napoleonic Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars). Following the conclusion of the war Cape Town was permanently ceded to the United Kingdom in the [Anglo-Dutch Treaty of 1814](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Dutch_Treaty_of_1814).

The city became the capital of the newly formed [Cape Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Cape_Colony), whose territory expanded very substantially through the 1800s, partially as a result of [numerous wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_Wars) with the [amaXhosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_people) on the colony's eastern frontier. In 1833 slavery was [abolished in the colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_South_Africa#Abolition) freeing over 5500 slaves in the city, almost a third of the city's population at the time.[^cite_note-Martin_1836_113-33] The [Convict Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convict_Crisis) of 1849, marked by substantial civil upheaval, bolstered the push for self-governance in the Cape.[^cite_note-\:7-34][^cite_note-\:8-35]

With expansion came calls for greater independence from the UK, with the Cape attaining [its own parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Cape_of_Good_Hope) (1854) and a [locally accountable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responsible_government#Cape_Colony) prime minister (1872). Suffrage was established according to the non-racial [Cape Qualified Franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Qualified_Franchise).[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37]

During the 1850s and 1860s, additional plant species were introduced from Australia by the British authorities. Notably [rooikrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia_cyclops) was introduced to stabilise the sand of the [Cape Flats](251477.md) to allow for a road connecting the peninsula with the rest of the African continent[^cite_note-38] and [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus) was used to drain marshes.[^cite_note-39]

In 1859 the first railway line was built by the [Cape Government Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Government_Railways) and a system of railways rapidly expanded in the 1870s. The discovery of diamonds in [Griqualand West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griqualand_West) in 1867, and the [Witwatersrand Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witwatersrand_Gold_Rush) in 1886, prompted a flood of immigration into South Africa.[^cite_note-40] In 1895 the city's first public power station, the [Graaff Electric Lighting Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graaff_Electric_Lighting_Works), was opened.

Conflicts between the [Boer republics](364762.md) in the interior and the British colonial government resulted in the [Second Boer War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Boer_War) of 1899–1902. Britain's victory in this war led to the formation of a united South Africa. From 1891 to 1901, the city's population more than doubled from 67,000 to 171,000.[^cite_note-C1875-41]

As the 19th century came to an end, the economic and political dominance of Cape Town in the Southern Africa region during the 19th century started to give way to the dominance of Johannesburg and Pretoria in the 20th century.[^cite_note-AMad-42]

### South African period



An aerial photograph of the newly completed [Cape Town foreshore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreshore,_Cape_Town) in 1945. Visible in this photograph as the large area of empty land between the City Centre and the newly constructed harbour out of reclaimed land from [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay).

[^cite_note-sahris_sahra_org_za-43]

In 1910 Britain established the [Union of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_South_Africa) which unified the Cape Colony with the two defeated Boer Republics and the British [colony of Natal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colony_of_Natal). Cape Town became the legislative capital of the Union, and later of the [Republic of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_South_Africa).

By the time of the [1936 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_1936), Johannesburg had overtaken Cape Town as the largest city in the country.

In 1945 the expansion of the [Cape Town foreshore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreshore,_Cape_Town) was completed, adding an additional 194 ha (480 acres) to the [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), and by extension, the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl) region.[^cite_note-sahris_sahra_org_za-43]

#### Apartheid era

Prior to the mid-twentieth century Cape Town was one of the most racially integrated cities in South Africa.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45] In the [1948 national elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_South_African_general_election) the [National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Party_\(South_Africa\)) won on a platform of *[apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid)* (racial segregation) under the slogan of "[swart gevaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swart_gevaar)" (Afrikaans for "black danger"). This led to the erosion and eventual abolition of the [Cape's multiracial franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Qualified_Franchise).

In 1950 the apartheid government first introduced the [Group Areas Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_Areas_Act), which classified and segregated urban areas according to race. Formerly multi-racial suburbs of Cape Town were either purged of residents deemed unlawful by apartheid legislation, or demolished. The most infamous example of this in Cape Town was the suburb of [District Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Six,_Cape_Town). After it was declared a whites-only area in 1965, all housing there was demolished and over 60,000 residents were forcibly removed.[^cite_note-46] Many of these residents were relocated to the [Cape Flats](251477.md).

The earliest of the Cape Flats forced removals saw the expulsion of Black South Africans to [Langa](3288563.md), Cape Town's first and oldest township, in line with the 1923 [Native Urban Areas Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natives_Urban_Areas_Act).

Under apartheid the Cape was considered a "[Coloured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coloured) labour preference area", to the exclusion of "[Bantus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu-speaking_peoples_of_South_Africa)", i.e. Black Africans. The implementation of this policy was widely opposed by trade unions, civil society and opposition parties. It is notable that this policy was not advocated for by any Coloured political group, and its implementation was a unilateral decision by the apartheid government.[^cite_note-47]

During the student-led [Soweto Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soweto_Uprising) of June 1976, school students from [Langa](3288563.md), [Gugulethu](4240611.md) and [Nyanga](7169539.md) in Cape Town reacted to the news of the protests against Bantu Education by organising gatherings and marches of their own. A number of school buildings were burnt down and the protest action was met with forceful resistance from the police.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49]

Cape Town has been home to many leaders of the [anti-apartheid movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_resistance_to_apartheid). In [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay), 10 km (6 mi) from the city is [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island). This [penitentiary island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximum_Security_Prison,_Robben_Island) was the site of a maximum security prison where many famous apartheid-era political prisoners served long prison sentences. Famous prisoners include activist, lawyer and future president [Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela) who served 18 of his 27 years of imprisonment on the island, as well as two other future presidents, [Kgalema Motlanthe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kgalema_Motlanthe) and [Jacob Zuma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Zuma).[^cite_note-50]

In one of the most famous moments marking the end of apartheid, Nelson Mandela made his first public speech since his imprisonment from the balcony of [Cape Town City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_City_Hall), hours after being released on 11 February 1990. His speech heralded the beginning of a new era for the country. The [first democratic election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_general_election,_1994) was held four years later on 27 April 1994.[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53]

[Nobel Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Square) in the [Victoria & Alfred Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_&_Alfred_Waterfront) features statues of South Africa's four [Nobel Peace Prize](26230922.md) winners: [Albert Luthuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Luthuli), [Desmond Tutu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desmond_Tutu), [F. W. de Klerk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F._W._de_Klerk) and [Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelson_Mandela).

#### Post-apartheid era

Cape Town has undergone significant changes in the years since Apartheid. Cape Town has experienced economic growth and development in the post-apartheid era. The city has become a major economic hub in South Africa, attracting international investment and tourism. The [Democratic Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Alliance_\(South_Africa\)) (DA), a [liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism) political party which came to power in Cape Town in 2006, has been credited with improving bureaucratic efficiency, public safety and fostering economic development.[^cite_note-\:0-54][^cite_note-\:1-55]

Opinion polls show that South Africans view the Western Cape as the best governed province, Cape Town as the best governed city in the country.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57] Of South Africa's 257 municipalities, only 38 received a clean financial audit in 2022 from the [Auditor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auditor-General_\(South_Africa\)). Of those, 21 were in the Western Cape.[^cite_note-\:0-54]

The city's economy has diversified, with growth in sectors such as technology, finance, real estate, and tourism. The establishment of the [Cape Town CCID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CCID) has been particularly successful in revitalizing the city center, bringing businesses and people back into the area. This initiative has transformed public spaces such as [Greenmarket Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenmarket_Square), [Company's Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company's_Garden), and St George's Mall, attracting both locals and tourists.[^cite_note-58]

In 2014 Cape Town was named [World Design Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Design_Capital) of the Year.[^cite_note-59] Cape Town was voted the best tourist destination in Africa at the 2023 World Travel Awards in [Dubai](211583.md) and continues to be the most important tourist destination in the country.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61] Cape Town has been named the best travel city in the world every year since 2013 in the [Telegraph](23797577.md) Travel Awards.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63]

Rapid urbanization driven by migration from the Northern and Eastern Cape,[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-\:5-65] along with the legacy of apartheid's spatial planning, have caused significant disparities between affluent areas and impoverished [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Township).[^cite_note-\:5-65][^cite_note-\:2-66] 60% of the city's population live in townships and informal settlements far from the city centre.[^cite_note-67] The legacy of Apartheid means Cape Town remains one of the most racially segregated cities in South Africa.[^cite_note-68] Many Black South Africans continue to live in informal settlements with limited access to basic services such as healthcare, education, and sanitation.[^cite_note-\:2-66][^cite_note-\:3-69]

The unemployment rate in Cape Town remains high at 23% as of 2024 (though nearly 10 points lower than the nationwide average), particularly among historically disadvantaged groups, and economic opportunities are unevenly distributed.[^cite_note-\:1-55]

Cape Town faced a [severe water shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_water_crisis) from 2015 to 2018.[^cite_note-70] According to [Oxfam](19698439.md), "in the face of an imminent water shortage, the city of Cape Town in South Africa successfully reduced its water use by more than half in three years, cutting it from 1.2bn litres per day in February 2015 to 516m litres per day in 2018."[^cite_note-71]

In 2021 Cape Town also experienced a [violent turf war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Cape_Town_taxi_conflict) between [rival mini-bus taxi firms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxi_wars_in_South_Africa) which led to the deaths of 83 people. Two years later [a strike by the mini-bus taxi firms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Cape_Town_taxi_strike) resulted in 5 deaths.

Since the 2010s, Cape Town and the wider Western Cape province have seen the rise of a small [secessionist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Independence).[^cite_note-72] Support for parties "which have formally adopted Cape independence" was around 5% in the [2021 municipal elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_South_African_municipal_elections).[^cite_note-73]

## Geography and the natural environment



[Devil's Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil's_Peak_\(Cape_Town\)), [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) and [Lion's Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion's_Head_\(Cape_Town\))



Satellite image of Cape Town showing the [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula) (left), [Cape Flats](251477.md) and [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay)



3D satellite image of the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl), containing [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), and [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain)

Cape Town is located at latitude 33.55° S (approximately the same as [Sydney](27862.md) and [Buenos Aires](255919.md) and equivalent to [Casablanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casablanca) and [Los Angeles](18110.md) in the northern hemisphere) and longitude 18.25° E.

[Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain), with its near vertical cliffs and flat-topped summit over 1,000 m (3,300 ft) high, and with [Devil's Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil's_Peak_\(Cape_Town\)) and [Lion's Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion's_Head_\(Cape_Town\)) on either side, together form a dramatic mountainous backdrop, which creates the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl). This area comprises the [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) as well as numerous suburbs on the mountainside.

A thin strip of cloud, known colloquially as the "tablecloth" ("Karos" in [Afrikaans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans)), sometimes forms on top of the mountain. To the immediate south of the city, the [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula) is a scenic mountainous spine jutting 40 km (25 mi) southward into the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) and terminating at [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point).

There are over 70 peaks above 300 m (980 ft) within [Cape Town's official metropolitan limits](4043753.md). Many of the city's suburbs lie on the large plain called the [Cape Flats](251477.md), which extends over 50 km (30 mi) to the east and joins the peninsula to the mainland. The Cape Town region is characterised by an extensive coastline, rugged mountain ranges, coastal plains and inland valleys.

### Extent

The extent of Cape Town has varied considerably over time. It originated as a small settlement at the foot of Table Mountain and has grown beyond its city limits as a [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area) to encompass the entire [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula) to the south, the [Cape Flats](251477.md), the [Helderberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helderberg) basin and part of the Steenbras catchment area to the east, and the Tygerberg hills, Blouberg and other areas to the north. [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island) in Table Bay is also part of Cape Town. It is bounded by the Atlantic Ocean to the west, and False Bay to the south. To the north and east, the extent is demarcated by boundaries of neighbouring municipalities within the Western Cape province.

The official boundaries of the city proper extend between the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl) and the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town) to the east and the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town) to the south. The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md), the [metropolitan municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality) that takes its name from the city covers the Greater Cape Town [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area), known as the Cape Metropole, extending beyond the city proper itself to include a number of satellite towns, suburbs and rural areas such as [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis,_South_Africa), [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_South_Africa), [Blouberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blouberg,_Western_Cape), [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell), [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville), [Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwood,_Western_Cape), [Gordon's Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon's_Bay), [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay), [Khayelitsha](2766632.md), [Kraaifontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraaifontein), [Kuilsrivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuils_River), [Macassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macassar,_Western_Cape), [Melkbosstrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkbosstrand), [Milnerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milnerton), [Muizenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muizenberg), [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town), [Parow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parow,_South_Africa), [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia,_South_Africa), [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town), [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West), and [Strand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strand,_Western_Cape) among others.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75]

The [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula) is 52 km (32 mi) long from [Mouille Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouille_Point) in the north to Cape Point in the south,[^cite_note-Map_3318-76] with an area of about 470 km<sup>2</sup> (180 sq mi), and it displays more topographical variety than other similar sized areas in southern Africa, and consequently spectacular scenery. There are diverse low-nutrient soils, large rocky outcrops, scree slopes, a mainly rocky coastline with embayed beaches, and considerable local variation in climatic conditions.[^cite_note-Cowling_et_al_1996-77]

The sedimentary rocks of the [Cape Supergroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Supergroup), of which parts of the Graafwater and Peninsula Formations remain, were uplifted between 280 and 21S million years ago, and were largely eroded away during the Mesozoic. The region was geologically stable during the Tertiary, which has led to slow denudation of the durable sandstones. Erosion rate and drainage has been influenced by fault lines and fractures, leaving remnant steep-sided massifs like Table Mountain surrounded by flatter slopes of deposits of the eroded material overlaying the older rocks,[^cite_note-Cowling_et_al_1996-77]

There are two internationally notable landmarks, [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) and [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point), at opposite ends of the Peninsula Mountain Chain, with the [Cape Flats](251477.md) and [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) to the east and the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) to the west. The landscape is dominated by sandstone plateaux and ridges, which generally drop steeply at their margins to the surrounding debris slopes, interrupted by a major gap at the Fish Hoek–Noordhoek valley.

In the south much of the area is a low sandstone plateau with sand dunes. Maximum altitude is 1,113 metres (3,652 ft) on Table Mountain.[^cite_note-Cowling_et_al_1996-77] The Cape Flats (Afrikaans: Kaapse Vlakte) is a flat, low-lying, sandy area, area to the east the Cape Peninsula, and west of the Helderberg much of which was wetland and dunes within recent history. To the north are the Tygerberg Hills and the Stellenbosch district.

The [Helderberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helderberg) area of Greater Cape Town, previously known as the "Hottentots-Holland" area, is mostly residential, but also a wine-producing area east of the Cape Flats, west of the Hottentots Holland mountain range and south of the Helderberg mountain, from which it gets its current name. The Helderberg consists of the previous municipalities of Somerset West, Strand, Gordons Bay and a few other towns. Industry and commerce is largely in service of the area.

After the Cape Peninsula, Helderberg is the next most mountainous part of Greater Cape Town, bordered to the north and east by the highest peaks in the region along the watershed of the Helderberg and Hottentots Holland Mountains, which are part of the Cape Fold Belt with [Cape Supergroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Supergroup) strata on a basement of Tygerberg Formation rocks intruded by part of the Stellenbosch granite pluton.

The region includes the entire catchment of the Lourens and Sir Lowry's rivers, separated by the Schapenberg hill, and a small part of the catchment of the Eerste River to the west. The Helderberg is ecologically highly diverse, rivaling the Cape Peninsula, and has its own endemic ecoregions and several conservation areas.

To the east of the Hottentots Holland mountains is the valley of the Steenbras River, in which the [Steenbras Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steenbras_Dam) was built as a water supply for Cape Town. The dam has been supplemented by several other dams around the western Cape, some of them considerably larger. This is almost entirely a conservation area, of high biodiversity. [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_South_Africa), [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell), [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville), [Kraaifontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraaifontein), [Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwood,_Western_Cape) and [Parow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parow,_South_Africa) are a few of the towns that make up the [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town) of Cape Town. In current popular culture these areas are often referred to as being beyond the "[boerewors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boerewors) curtain," a play on the term "[iron curtain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_curtain)."

[UNESCO](21786641.md) declared [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island) in the Western Cape a [World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Africa) in 1999. Robben Island is located in Table Bay, some 6 km (3.7 mi) west of Bloubergstrand, a coastal suburb north of Cape Town, and stands some 30 metres (98 ft) above sea level. Robben Island has been used as a [prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximum_Security_Prison,_Robben_Island) where people were isolated, banished, and exiled for nearly 400 years. It was also used as a [leper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leprosy) colony, a post office, a grazing ground, a mental hospital, and an outpost.[^cite_note-78]

The coastline of Cape Town is roughly 307 km long, from Silwerstroomstrand at [33°34′14.994″S 18°20′34.959″E  /  33.57083167°S 18.34304417°E  / -33.57083167; 18.34304417](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Cape_Town\&params=18_20_34.959_E_33_34_14.994_S_) on the west coast to slightly south of Kogelbaai at [34°16′10.554″S 18°50′44.905″E  /  34.26959833°S 18.84580694°E  / -34.26959833; 18.84580694](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Cape_Town\&params=18_50_44.905_E_34_16_10.554_S_) on the east coast of False Bay.[^cite_note-79]



Cape Town's [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl) and [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) viewed from [Lion's Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion's_Head_\(Cape_Town\)) in May (late autumn)



Cape Town with surrounding peaks

### Geology

Main article: [Geology of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Cape_Town)



Geological map of the Cape Peninsula and False Bay

The [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula) is a rocky and mountainous [peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula) that juts out into the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) at the south-western extremity of the continent. At its tip is Cape Point and the [Cape of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_of_Good_Hope). The peninsula forms the west side of False Bay and the Cape Flats. On the east side are the Helderberg and Hottentots Holland mountains.

The three main rock formations are the late-[Precambrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precambrian) Malmesbury group (sedimentary and metamorphic rock), the Cape Granite suit, comprising the huge Peninsula, Kuilsrivier-Helderberg, and Stellenbosch batholiths, that were intruded into the Malmesbury Group about 630 million years ago, and the [Table Mountain group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Fold_Belt#Table_Mountain_group) sandstones that were deposited on the eroded surface of the granite and Malmesbury series basement about 450 million years ago.

The sand, silt and mud deposits were lithified by pressure and then folded during the Cape Orogeny to form the [Cape Fold Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Fold_Belt), which extends in an arc along the western and southern coasts. The present landscape is due to prolonged erosion having carved out deep valleys, removing parts of the once continuous Table Mountain Group sandstone cover from over the Cape Flats and False Bay, and leaving high residual mountain ridges.[^cite_note-Compton_2004-80]

At times the sea covered the Cape Flats and [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town) valley and the Cape Peninsula was then a group of islands. During glacial periods the sea level dropped to expose the bottom of False Bay to weathering and erosion, with the last major regression leaving the entire bottom of False Bay exposed.

During this period an extensive system of dunes was formed on the sandy floor of False Bay. At this time the drainage outlets lay between Rocky Bank Cape Point to the west, and between Rocky Bank and Hangklip Ridge to the east, with the watershed roughly along the line of the contact zone east of Seal Island and Whittle Rock.[^cite_note-Compton_2004-80][^cite_note-Brown_and_Magoba_2009_part_1-81]<sup>: Ch2 </sup>

### Climate



[Llandudno, Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llandudno,_Western_Cape) during a sunny day

Cape Town has a [hot-summer Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot-summer_Mediterranean_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Csa*),[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84] with mild, moderately wet winters and dry, hot summers. Winter, which lasts from June to September, may see large cold fronts entering for limited periods from the Atlantic Ocean with significant [precipitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precipitation_\(meteorology\)) and strong north-westerly winds. Winter months in the city average a maximum of 18 °C (64 °F) and minimum of 8.5 °C (47 °F).

Winters are [snow](28191.md) and [frost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frost) free, except on Table Mountain and on other mountain peaks, where light accumulation of snow and frost can sometimes occur.[^cite_note-WMO-85] Total annual rainfall in the city averages 515 mm (20.3 in) although in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town), close to the mountains, rainfall is significantly higher and averages closer to 1,000 mm (39.4 in).

Summer, which lasts from December to March, is warm and dry with an average maximum of 26 °C (79 °F) and minimum of 16 °C (61 °F). The region can get uncomfortably hot when the [Berg Wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berg_Wind), meaning "mountain wind", blows from the [Karoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karoo) interior.

Spring and summer generally feature a strong wind from the south-east, known locally as the south-easter or the [Cape Doctor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Doctor), so called because it blows air pollution away. This wind is caused by a persistent [high-pressure system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pressure_system#High-pressure_system) over the [South Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Atlantic_Ocean) to the west of Cape Town, known as the [South Atlantic High](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Atlantic_High), which shifts latitude seasonally, following the sun, and influencing the strength of the fronts and their northward reach. Cape Town receives about 3,100 hours of sunshine per year.[^cite_note-NOAA-86]

Water temperatures range greatly, between 10 °C (50 °F) on the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town), to over 22 °C (72 °F) in [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay). Average annual ocean surface temperatures are between 13 °C (55 °F) on the Atlantic Seaboard (similar to Californian waters, such as [San Francisco](49728.md) or [Big Sur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Sur)), and 17 °C (63 °F) in False Bay (similar to Northern Mediterranean temperatures, such as [Nice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nice) or [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo)).

Unlike other parts of the country, the city does not have many [thunderstorms](70807.md), and most of those that do occur happen around October to December and March to April.

#### Climate change

A 2019 paper published in [PLOS One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLOS_One) estimated that under [Representative Concentration Pathway 4.5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_Concentration_Pathway#4.5), a "moderate" scenario of [climate change](5042951.md) where global warming reaches \~2.5–3 °C (4.5–5.4 °F) by 2100, the climate of Cape Town in the year 2050 would most closely resemble the current climate of [Perth](24355.md) in Western Australia.

The annual temperature would increase by 1.1 °C (2.0 °F), and the temperature of the coldest month by 0.3 °C (0.54 °F), while the temperature of the warmest month would be 2.3 °C (4.1 °F) higher.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] According to [Climate Action Tracker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_Action_Tracker), the current warming trajectory appears consistent with 2.7 °C (4.9 °F), which closely matches RCP 4.5.[^cite_note-93]

Moreover, according to the 2022 [IPCC Sixth Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPCC_Sixth_Assessment_Report), Cape Town is one of 12 major African cities ([Abidjan](21371767.md), [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria), [Algiers](1644.md), Cape Town, [Casablanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casablanca), [Dakar](44251.md), [Dar es Salaam](8500.md), [Durban](313650.md), [Lagos](85232.md), [Lomé](57067.md), [Luanda](18402.md) and [Maputo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maputo)) which would be the most severely affected by future [sea level rise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level_rise). It estimates that they would collectively sustain cumulative damages of US\$65 billion under RCP 4.5 and US\$86.5 billion for the high-emission scenario RCP 8.5 by the year 2050.

Additionally, RCP 8.5 combined with the hypothetical impact from [marine ice sheet instability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_ice_sheet_instability) at high levels of warming would involve up to US\$137.5 billion in damages, while the additional accounting for the "low-probability, high-damage events" may increase aggregate risks to US\$187 billion for the "moderate" RCP4.5, US\$206 billion for RCP8.5 and US\$397 billion under the high-end ice sheet instability scenario.[^cite_note-94]

Since sea level rise would continue for about 10,000 years under every scenario of climate change, future costs of sea level rise would only increase, especially without adaptation measures.[^cite_note-95]

### Hydrology

See also: [Cape Peninsula § Drainage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula#Drainage), [Helderberg § Drainage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helderberg#Drainage), and [Cape Flats § Drainage](251477.md#Drainage)

#### Sea surface temperatures

See also: [False Bay § Waves, tides, water circulation and temperature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay#Waves,_tides,_water_circulation_and_temperature); and [Cape Peninsula § Oceanography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula#Oceanography)



Charts of sea surface temperature in and near False Bay in summer and winter

[^cite_note-Pfaff_et_al_2019-96]

Cape Town's coastal water ranges from cold to mild, and the difference between the two sides of the peninsula can be dramatic. While the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town) averages annual sea surface temperatures average between 10 and 15 °C (50 and 59 °F), the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) coast is much warmer, averaging between 16 and 22 °C (61 and 72 °F) annually.[^cite_note-City_and_Beaches_of_Capetown-97]

In summer, [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) water averages slightly over 20 °C (68 °F), with 22 °C (72 °F) an occasional high. Beaches located on the Atlantic Coast tend to have colder water due to the wind driven upwellings which contribute to the [Benguela Current](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benguela_Current) which originates off the Cape Peninsula, while the water at False Bay beaches may occasionally be warmer by up to 10 °C (18 °F) at the same time in summer.[^cite_note-City_and_Beaches_of_Capetown-97]

In summer False Bay is thermally stratified, with a vertical temperature variation of 5 to 9˚C between the warmer surface water and cooler depths below 50 m, while in winter the water column is at nearly constant temperature at all depths. The development of a [thermocline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermocline) is strongest around late December and peaks in late summer to early autumn.[^cite_note-Coleman_2019-98]<sup>: 8 </sup> In summer the south easterly winds generate a zone of upwelling near [Cape Hangklip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pringle_Bay), where surface water temperatures can be 6 to 7 °C colder than the surrounding areas, and bottom temperatures below 12 °C.[^cite_note-Coleman_2019-98]<sup>: 10 </sup>

In the summer to early autumn (January–March), cold water upwelling near Cape Hangklip causes a strong surface temperature gradient between the south-western and north-eastern corners of the bay. In winter the surface temperature tends to be much the same everywhere. In the northern sector surface temperature varies a bit more (13 to 22 °C) than in the south (14 to 20 °C) during the year.[^cite_note-Pfaff_et_al_2019-96]

Surface temperature variation from year to year is linked to the [El Niño–Southern Oscillation](317311.md). During El Niño years the South Atlantic high is shifted, reducing the south-easterly winds, so upwelling and evaporative cooling are reduced and sea surface temperatures throughout the bay are warmer, while in La Niña years there is more wind and upwelling and consequently lower temperatures. Surface water heating during El Niño increases vertical stratification. The relationship is not linear.[^cite_note-Pfaff_et_al_2019-96] Occasionally eddies from the Agulhas current will bring warmer water and vagrant sea life carried from the south and east coasts into False Bay.

### Flora and fauna

Main article: [Biodiversity of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_of_Cape_Town)



[Peninsula Sandstone Fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_Sandstone_Fynbos) growing in [Table Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_National_Park).

Located in a [Conservation International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservation_International) [biodiversity hotspot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot) as well as the unique [Cape Floristic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region), the city of Cape Town has one of the highest levels of [biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity) of any equivalent area in the world.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-fri1-100]

These protected areas are a [World Heritage Site](44940.md), and an estimated 2,200 species of plants are confined to Table Mountain – more than exist in the whole of the [United Kingdom](31717.md) which has 1200 plant species and 67 endemic plant species.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] Many of these species, including a great many types of [proteas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protea), are endemic to the mountain and can be found nowhere else.[^cite_note-104]

It is home to a total of 19 different [vegetation types](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phytochorion), of which several are [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic) to the city and occur nowhere else in the world.[^cite_note-105] It is also the only habitat of hundreds of endemic species,[^cite_note-106] and hundreds of others which are severely restricted or threatened. This enormous species diversity is mainly because the city is uniquely located at the convergence point of several different soil types and micro-climates.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]

Table Mountain has an unusually rich biodiversity. Its vegetation consists predominantly of several different types of the unique and rich Cape [Fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fynbos). The main vegetation type is endangered [Peninsula Sandstone Fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_Sandstone_Fynbos), but [critically endangered](7451605.md) [Peninsula Granite Fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_Granite_Fynbos), [Peninsula Shale Renosterveld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_Shale_Renosterveld) and [Afromontane forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afromontane) occur in smaller portions on the mountain.

Rapid [population growth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_growth) and [urban sprawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanisation) has covered much of these ecosystems with development. Consequently, Cape Town now has over 300 [threatened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Threatened) plant species and 13 which are now [extinct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extinct). The [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula), which lies entirely within the city of Cape Town, has the highest concentration of [threatened species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Threatened_species) of any continental area of equivalent size in the world.[^cite_note-109]

Tiny remnant populations of [critically endangered](7451605.md) or [near extinct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extinct) plants sometimes survive on road sides, pavements and sports fields.[^cite_note-110] The remaining ecosystems are partially protected through a system of over 30 [nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_nature_reserves_in_Cape_Town) – including the massive [Table Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_National_Park).[^cite_note-111]

Cape Town reached first place in the 2019 iNaturalist City Nature Challenge in two out of the three categories: Most Observations, and Most Species. This was the first entry by Capetonians in this annual competition to observe and record the local biodiversity over a four-day long weekend during what is considered the worst time of the year for local observations.[^cite_note-Two_Oceans-112]

A worldwide survey suggested that the extinction rate of endemic plants from the City of Cape Town is one of the highest in the world, at roughly three per year since 1900 – partly a consequence of the very small and localised habitats and high endemicity.[^cite_note-Rebelo_2019-113]

In 2025, City of Cape Town enacted the *Cape Town Biodiversity Spatial Plan (CTBSP) 2025*, a biodiversity policy aimed at guiding development in the city. The plan integrates the Cape Town BioNet 2024 Map, which identifies areas to protect to ensure environmental sustainability, and categorizes them into Protected Areas, Critical Biodiversity Areas, Ecological Support Areas, and Other Natural Areas. The policy will help guide spatial planning, environmental assessments, and natural resource management across sectors, and sets a framework for the internal alignment of environmental responsibilities across all City departments.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_adopts_new_biodiversity_plan_to_guide_development-114]

22.72% of Cape Town's municipal land, totaling 55,697 hectares, is under conservation. Of this, 20,039 hectares are managed by the City within 22 nature reserves and 16 Biodiversity Agreement Conservation Areas, many of which are open to the public.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_adopts_new_biodiversity_plan_to_guide_development-114]

Cape Town is governed by a 231-member city council elected in a system of [mixed-member proportional representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed-member_proportional_representation). The city is divided into 116 [wards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ward_\(South_Africa\)), each of which elects a councillor by [first-past-the-post voting](261709.md). The remaining 115 councillors are elected from [party lists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_list) so that the total number of councillors for each party is proportional to the number of votes received by that party.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116]

In the [2021 Municipal Elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_South_African_municipal_elections), the Democratic Alliance (DA) kept its majority, this time diminished, taking 136 seats. The African National Congress lost substantially, receiving 43 of the seats.[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] The Democratic Alliance candidate for the Cape Town mayoralty, [Geordin Hill-Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geordin_Hill-Lewis) was elected mayor.[^cite_note-119]

In recent years, Cape Town has developed a reputation for good governance, with the city's [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure) spending and upkeep, political stability, CBD [safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety), and municipal service delivery being generally regarded as the best out of all the major metropolitan areas in South Africa. In 2025, Cape Town was, for the third year in a row, the only metropolitan municipality in South Africa to receive a clean [audit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audit) outcome from its [Auditor General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auditor-General_\(South_Africa\)) (AG), Tsakani Maluleke.[^cite_note-The_best-run_city_in_South_Africa_for_three_years_in_a_row-120]

According to the AG, municipalities that achieve clean audits display strong financial and performance management, whilst adhering to legislative requirements, and Cape Town is a model of governance and accountability. The AG further stated that Cape Town showed strong leadership, skilled personnel, accurate records, credible reporting to communities, and good quality planning and performance management. The city has maintained its clean audit status since 2021.[^cite_note-The_best-run_city_in_South_Africa_for_three_years_in_a_row-120]

### Budget

Municipal budgets in SA are set every 3 years, according to the Medium-Term Revenue and Expenditure Framework (MTREF). Some metros, including Cape Town, announce their annual budgets as well.[^cite_note-National_Treasury_on_Medium-Term_Expenditure_Framework_Technical_Guidelines_2025-121]

The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) tabled a roughly R86 billion budget for its 2026/2027 financial year, equating to around a 2-3% increase year-over-year, in line with inflation.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_trims_rates,_caps_tariff_hikes_in_latest_budget-122] Cape Town has one of the highest per capita budgets out of all SA metros, and in the 2025/2026 financial year, its total budget constituted around 20% of the total combined budgets of SA's eight metros.

Approximately R73 billion for that same year was allocated towards the City's operating budget (service delivery), with a further approximately R13 billion going towards its capital budget (infrastructure development). The City spends significantly on infrastructure, in line with its commitment to spend R40 billion over three years to improve various aspects of its infrastructure. A large portion of that spending has in recent years gone towards low-income areas.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_trims_rates,_caps_tariff_hikes_in_latest_budget-122]

For the 2025/2026 financial year, focus areas for the City's capital budget included the [MyCiTi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyCiTi) BRT Phase 2 expansion, water network upgrades, expanding the SSU CCTV network, deploying more Metro Police officers, and building small scale energy plants. The City also incorporated a significant social package as part of that year's budget, including adjusted rebates for homes under a certain value. Furthermore, the City absorbed the majority of an 11% electricity tariff placed on metros by [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom), increasing rates for Cape Town residents by just 2%.[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_Budget_2025/2026-123]

#### Allocation of rates

Cape Town has the lowest [rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax_in_South_Africa#Rates) out of all South African metros. However, naturally, due to the size of Cape Town's population and the value of its real estate market, significant rates are raised each year. The City details how it allocates those rates. For the 2025/2026 budget, the City stated that for every R1 in rates paid, the allocation was as follows:[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_Budget_2025/2026-123]

- 29% to law enforcement, metro police, fire, and traffic services

- 15% to free and subsidized services

- 15% to customer care, IT, and service delivery

- 14% to infrastructure investment and human settlements

- 13% to parks, the environment, and libraries

- 6% to economic growth, tourism, and informal trading

- 4% to city clinics and health services

- 4% to the [MyCiTi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyCiTi) public transport network

#### 2022 invasion of Ukraine



Cape Town City hall lit up in the colours of the Ukrainian flag as a gesture of solidarity with the country.

The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) has expressed explicit support for Ukraine during the [2022 invasion of the country by Russia](79400268.md).[^cite_note-125] To show this support the City of Cape Town lit up the Old City Hall in the colours of the [Ukrainian flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Ukraine) on 2 March 2022.[^cite_note-Evans_2022-126][^cite_note-127] This has differentiated the city from the officially [neutral foreign policy position](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations#2022_Russian_invasion) taken by the [South African national government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_South_Africa).[^cite_note-Evans_2022-126]

Cape Town population pyramid in 2011



Population density in Cape Town

According to the [South African National Census of 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_2011), the population of the City of Cape Town metropolitan municipality – an area that includes suburbs and [exurbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exurb) – is 3,740,026 people. This represents an annual growth rate of 2.6% compared to the results of the [previous census in 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_2001) which found a population of 2,892,243 people.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137] <sup>: 54 </sup>

Of those residents who were asked about their [first language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_language), 35.7% spoke [Afrikaans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans), 29.8% spoke [Xhosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_language) and 28.4% spoke [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_English). 24.8% of the population is under the age of 15, while 5.5% is 65 or older.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 64 </sup> The [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) is 0.96, meaning that there are slightly more women than men.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 55 </sup>

Of those residents aged 20 or older, 1.8% have no schooling, 8.1% have some schooling but did not finish primary school, 4.6% finished primary school but have no secondary schooling, 38.9% have some secondary schooling but did not finish [Grade 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grade_12), 29.9% finished Grade 12 but have no higher education, and 16.7% have higher education.

Overall, 46.6% have at least a Grade 12 education.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 74 </sup> Of those aged between 5 and 25, 67.8% are attending an educational institution.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 78 </sup> Amongst those aged between 15 and 65 the unemployment rate is 23.7%.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 79 </sup>

The average annual household income in the [Western Cape](313646.md), of which Cape Town is the capital and largest city, is South Africa's highest, by a large margin. As of June 2025, the province's average household income is [R](298686.md)407,000. This amount is 35% higher than second-place [Gauteng](309661.md), and 50% above the national average.[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22]

The total number of households grew from 653,085 in 1996 to 1,068,572 in 2011, which represents an increase of 63.6%.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 81 </sup> The average number of household members declined from 3,92 in 1996 to 3,50 in 2011.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_2016-138] Of those households, 78.4% are in formal structures (houses or [flats](265567.md)), while 20.5% are in informal structures ([shacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanty_town)).[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 81 </sup>

97.3% of City-supplied households have access to electricity,[^cite_note-139] and 94.0% of households use [electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mains_electricity) for lighting.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 84 </sup> 87.3% of households have [piped water](684928.md) to the dwelling, while 12.0% have piped water through a communal tap.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 85 </sup> 94.9% of households have regular [refuse collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refuse_collection) service.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 86 </sup>

91.4% of households have a [flush toilet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flush_toilet) or [chemical toilet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_toilet), while 4.5% still use a bucket toilet.[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]<sup>: 87 </sup> 82.1% of households have a [refrigerator](1036259.md), 87.3% have a television and 70.1% have a radio. Only 34.0% have a landline telephone, but 91.3% have a cellphone. 37.9% have a computer, and 49.3% have access to the Internet (either through a computer or a cellphone).[^cite_note-wc-muni-report-137]

In 2011 over 70% of cross provincial South African migrants coming into the Western Cape settled in Cape Town; 53.64% of South African migrants into the Western Cape came from the [Eastern Cape](313645.md), the old Cape Colony's former native reserve, and 20.95% came from Gauteng province.[^cite_note-140]

Cape Town has a young population. In the 2022 [census](6889.md), the median age of residents in the city was 31. In the same year, approximately 40% of the population of Cape Town was under the age of 25.[^cite_note-2022_Census_CCT_Profile-141]

According to the 2016 City of Cape Town community survey, there were 4,004,793 people in the City of Cape Town metro. Out of this population, 45.7% identified as Black African, 35.1% identified as Coloured, 16.2% identified as White and 1.6% identified as Asian.[^cite_note-142]

The Western Cape province, in which Cape Town is the largest city, has the highest rate of marriage out of all South African provinces, by a significant margin. In the 2022 census, 33.8% of those in the province were legally married – almost 10% higher than the next province. In the same census, 50.6% of Western Cape residents had never been married – the lowest out of all South African provinces.[^cite_note-Census_2022_-_Statistical_Release-143]

During the outbreak of the [COVID-19 pandemic in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_South_Africa), local media reported that increasing numbers of wealthy and middle-class South Africans have started moving from inland areas of the country to coastal regions – most notably Cape Town – in a phenomenon referred to as "*semigration*" – short for "semi-emigration"[^cite_note-Staff_Writer-144][^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] Declining municipal services in the rest of the country and the [South African energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_energy_crisis) are other cited reasons for semigration.[^cite_note-147]

In 2022, the city expected its population to grow by an additional 400,000 residents between 2020 and 2025, with 76% of those new residents falling into the low-income bracket, earning less than [R](298686.md)13,000 a month.[^cite_note-148]

According to the United Nations' World Urbanization Prospects (2018), Cape Town's population is projected to continue growing steadily over the next decade. Based on estimates compiled by the Department of Economic and Social Affairs in 2018, the city's population is expected to reach approximately 5.468 million by 2030, and roughly 5.845 million by 2035.[^cite_note-UNWUP2018-149]

### Religion

See also: [Religion in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_South_Africa)



[St George's Anglican Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._George's_Cathedral,_Cape_Town) is one of the largest and oldest religious sites in the city.

In the [2022 South African Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_South_African_Census), religious data were gathered for the [Western Cape](313646.md) province, of which Cape Town is the capital and largest city (with 64.21% of the province's population residing in the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) metro area). The Western Cape census respondents self-identified as follows:[^cite_note-Census_2022_Statistical_Release-151]

- 85.6% as [Christian](18337522.md)

- 5.2% as [Muslim](19541.md)

- 5.3% as following a [traditional African religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_African_religions)

- 2% as having [no religious affiliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious)

- 0.8% as "other"

- 0.3% as [Agnostic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnostic)

- 0.3% as [Atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist)

- 0.2% as [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu)

- 0.2% as [Jewish](25955086.md)

- 0.1% as [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist)

As of 2022, a total of 2.6% of Western Cape residents are [irreligious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligious).[^cite_note-Census_2022_Statistical_Release-151]

Most [places of worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Places_of_worship) in Cape Town are Christian churches and cathedrals. These include the [Dutch Reformed Church in South Africa (NGK)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Reformed_Church_in_South_Africa_\(NGK\)), [Zion Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion_Christian_Church), [Apostolic Faith Mission of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Faith_Mission_of_South_Africa), [Assemblies of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assemblies_of_God), [Baptist Union of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist_Union_of_Southern_Africa) (Baptist World Alliance), [Methodist Church of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist_Church_of_Southern_Africa) (World Methodist Council), [Anglican Church of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Southern_Africa) (Anglican Communion), [Presbyterian Church of Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_of_Africa) (World Communion of Reformed Churches), [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Cape_Town) (Catholic Church),[^cite_note-152] the [Orthodox Archbishopric of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Patriarchate_of_Alexandria) ([Greek Orthodox Cathedral of St George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Cathedral_of_St_George)),[^cite_note-153] and [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) (LDS Church).[^cite_note-154]

[Islam](6037917.md) is the city's second largest religion with a [long history in Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_South_Africa).[^cite_note-155] This has resulted in a number of mosques and other Muslim religious sites spread across the city,[^cite_note-156] such as the [Auwal Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auwal_Mosque) - South Africa's first mosque.

Cape Town's significant [Jewish population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_South_Africa) supports a number of synagogues, the most notable of which is the historic [Gardens Shul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardens_Shul), the oldest Jewish congregation in South Africa.[^cite_note-forward-157] [Marais Road Shul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marais_Road_Shul), located in [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point) (a suburb with a notable Jewish population), is the largest Jewish congregation in South Africa.[^cite_note-158] [Temple Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_Israel_\(Cape_Town\)) (Cape Town Progressive Jewish Congregation) also has three temples in the city.[^cite_note-159] Other affiliated institutions include a [Chabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chabad) centre in Sea Point and a [Chabad on Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chabad_on_Campus_International_Foundation) at the [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town), catering to Jewish students.[^cite_note-160]

Other religious sites in the city include [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu) and [Buddhist](3267529.md) temples and centres.[^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162]

### Crime



Sign warning motorists about the prevalence of [smash and grab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smash_and_grab) incidents at a "Crime Hot Spot" on the [M5 freeway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M5_\(Cape_Town\)), between the lower-income neighbourhoods of [Grassy Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grassy_Park) and [Retreat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retreat,_Cape_Town), situated in the [Cape Flats](251477.md) region of the city

#### Overview

In recent years, the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) ([metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)) government) has made significant progress in combatting crime across Cape Town. Measures include the expanded use of digital tools, such as [CCTV cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_camera) and [UAVs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_unmanned_aerial_vehicles_in_law_enforcement); increased Highway Patrol presence; substantially increased numbers of Law Enforcement officers; dispatching specialized teams of officers to areas based on the types of localized crimes there; targeting areas of specifically high crime; and lobbying the [national government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_South_Africa) for more control (via the [devolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution) of powers) over local policing.[^cite_note-City_patrols_reshape_safety_picture_on_major_highways-163]

Measures also include facilitating collaboration between various local and provincial law enforcement agencies and units (including the Traffic Service, Highway Patrol, Flying Squad, Ghost Squad, Law Enforcement, LEAP, LEAS, Disaster Volunteers, Gang and Drug Task Team, Metal Theft Unit, and SSU).[^cite_note-City_patrols_reshape_safety_picture_on_major_highways-163]

Overall, Cape Town has historically had high levels of violent crime. However, these city-wide statistics are heavily skewed and predominantly driven by [gang violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_violence) in specific suburbs, many of which are located in the lower-income [Cape Flats](251477.md) region of the city.[^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164] This situation has created quite a disparity between safety levels amongst residents of Cape Town, with those in many of the city's suburbs enjoying a life devoid of the kinds of risks faced by those living in and commuting through high-crime neighbourhoods.[^cite_note-\:3-69][^cite_note-\:4-165][^cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166]

Crime in Cape Town in general is attributed to various factors, many of which are shared with other major [South African metros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)). These include [economic inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_inequality) (which can be linked to the legacy of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid)'s spatial and social divisions), [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment), [alcohol abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcohol_abuse), the prevalence of illegal [firearms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firearms), a lack of rehabilitation and support services for offenders leading to [recidivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recidivism), and a lack of community safety organizations.[^cite_note-\:3-69][^cite_note-\:4-165][^cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166]

Crime in Cape Town is a serious problem which affects the [quality of life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quality_of_life) and safety of its residents and visitors. Between 2022 and 2023, Cape Town recorded the highest number of murders in a single year of any city in the world, at 2,998, followed by fellow South African metros [Johannesburg](58893.md) and [Durban](313650.md). This represented an increase of 8.6% year-on-year.[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] Household crimes including [burglary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burglary) also increased in the same period.[^cite_note-169] In 2022, [Mexico](3966054.md)'s Citizen Council for Public Security and Criminal Justice ranked Cape Town among the most violent cities in the world, based on average crime statistics across all suburbs.[^cite_note-170]

The minibus taxi industry has been the source of a number of [violent confrontations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxi_wars_in_South_Africa) in the city. The northern and eastern sections of the city was the scene of the [2021 Cape Town taxi conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Cape_Town_taxi_conflict), a violent turf war which led to 83 deaths. The [2023 Cape Town taxi strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Cape_Town_taxi_strike) resulted in 5 recorded deaths.[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172]

#### Localization of crime

As with other major metropolitan areas in South Africa, the majority of crime in Cape Town tends to be concentrated in very specific areas, more often than not being the Cape Flats, indicating the potential success of place-based crime intelligence methods.[^cite_note-Violent_crime_in_South_Africa_happens_mostly_in_a_few_hotspots:_police_resources_should_focus_there_–_criminologist-166]

In Cape Town, a lot of the crime that occurs, especially violent crime, takes place in the neighbourhoods of [Philippi East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippi_East?action=edit\&redlink=1) (a notorious area for such crime), Delft, Nyanga, Mfuleni, Gugulethu, Elsies River, Bishop Lavis, Grassy Park, Lentegeur, Kleinvlei, and Mitchell's Plain. All of these suburbs are best avoided (even during transit) by locals and tourists.[^cite_note-Crime_stats:_Murder_down_nearly_10%,_but_an_average_of_75_people_killed_daily-173][^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164]

Other notable areas for violent crime include Kraaifontein, Atlantis, Athlone, Kleinvlei, Khayelitsha, Harare, Bishop Lavis, Muizenberg, Lingelethu-West, Table View, [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Western_Cape), Parow, Goodwood, Milnerton, and Makhaza. These suburbs are also best avoided (even during transit) by locals and tourists.[^cite_note-Crime_stats:_Murder_down_nearly_10%,_but_an_average_of_75_people_killed_daily-173][^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164]

Notable areas for high rates of non-violent crime include [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), Steenberg, Wynberg, Sea Point, Woodstock, Samora Machel, Claremont, Somerset West, Brackenfell, Ravensmead, Philippi, and Strand.[^cite_note-Recorded_Crime_Statistics_Republic_Of_South_Africa:_Second_Quarter_of_2025_to_2026_Financial_Year_(July_2025_to_September_2025)-164]

#### Travellers

While the [UK Foreign Office](59653.md) considers Cape Town safe to travel to, it notes high crime rates on certain roads leading to and from [Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport). These include the [R300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R300_\(South_Africa\)) regional road, and Borcherds Quarry Road. The Foreign Office recommends using the [M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M3_\(Cape_Town\)) metropolitan freeway and [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_\(South_Africa\)) national freeway instead of the R300, and recommends using the Airport Approach Road (Exit 16 on the N2), instead of Borcherds Quarry Road. Doing so allows drivers to avoid being routed to and through high-crime areas and intersections.[^cite_note-Foreign_Travel_Advice_-_South_Africa-174][^cite_note-FCDO_updates_safety_advice_for_travellers_to_South_Africa-175]

#### Community initiatives

For certain activities in which locals partake, members of the community have come together to increase safety in Cape Town. An example for [hiking](147313.md) - a popular pastime in Cape Town - is the Hikers Network's[^cite_note-The_Hikers_Network_-_Homepage-176] SafetyMountain Tracking, a [WhatsApp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WhatsApp)-based emergency service manned by volunteers. The service tracks hikers and other [mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain) users (such as [runners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running) and [cyclists](5931.md)) on their adventures, giving them instant access to assistance in case of an emergency, such as injury, sudden illness, rockfall, getting lost, and crime.[^cite_note-SafetyMountain_Tracking-177]

#### Recent improvements

In early 2025, the City of Cape Town announced that progress had been made in combatting crime, with crime levels in certain notorious areas decreasing, according to statistics based on community reporting of incidents of crime. Daily patrol officers were complimented for their efforts, and the City said it was working closely with the [Western Cape Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Government) and the [South African Police Service](1228194.md) to adjust its law enforcement efforts according to changes in the city's crime statistics.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_police_stations_lead_in_SA_murder_statistics-178]

Despite the crime levels, the city's [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy) has grown, as a result of good governance, investments in infrastructure, and an attractive business environment, as well as due to growth in the local [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism) and [real estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate) industries.[^cite_note-179] Since July 2019 widespread violent crime in poorer [gang dominated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangs_in_South_Africa) areas of greater Cape Town has resulted in an ongoing military presence in these neighbourhoods.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181]

In terms of minibus taxi safety, the [Western Cape Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Government) now takes to special measures during times of high crime, whereby it temporarily shuts down specific routes that are directly impacted. The Province is able to invoke Section 91 of the National Land Transport Act, a legal provision that allows a provincial government to impose extraordinary measures when violence, unrest, or instability in the public transport sector places lives at risk. Such measures were taken by the Western Cape Mobility Department for 30 days (with a subsequent extension), on routes in Mfuleni, Somerset West, Khayelitsha, Nomzamo, and Lwandle, in late 2025.[^cite_note-Western_Cape_Mobility_on_closure_of_certain_minibus_taxi_routes_in_response_to_ongoing_violence-182]

In 2025, Cape Town's Mayoral Committee Member (MCM) for Safety and Security, [JP Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JP_Smith_\(politician\)), said that the expanded use of digital tools to complement [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement) efforts has become part of the city's [public safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_safety) operations. These tools include a vast network of [CCTV cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCTV_camera) and [drones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Use_of_unmanned_aerial_vehicles_in_law_enforcement), which have already proven successful in assisting with both arrests and prosecution.[^cite_note-Large_South_African_city_using_cameras_and_drones_to_stop_crime-183]

## Economy



[Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) is the main economic hub of the city, housing the headquarters of many of South Africa's largest companies, as well as numerous tourist attractions and amenities for residents
