Cape Town is [Africa](5334607.md)'s third-largest economic hub, and South Africa's second-largest by annual gross metropolitan output. It serves as the regional financial and manufacturing center in the [Western Cape](313646.md) province.

The city has a diverse economy that is not significantly reliant on any particular sector. Major industries in Cape Town include tech, renewable energy, retail, finance, boat building, film, and conferences.

The financial sector accounts for 22% of the Western Cape's annual economic output, as of 2025.

Cape Town is considered a global [tech hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech_hub), and is home to numerous startups, startup incubators, and large tech companies.[^cite_note-Cape_Town:_Africa’s_Tech_Hub_on_the_Rise-185] In 2025, Cape Town's tech sector was growing at an annual rate of 8%.

The city also has a substantial Green Economy, with numerous [renewable energy](25784.md) companies headquartered in the metro, and around R15 billion invested into the sector in 2024 alone.[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22]

### Income levels

As of June 2025, Cape Town has South Africa's highest average household income, as well as the country's lowest rate of unemployment, and strongest-performing property market.[^cite_note-CT-26]

The average annual household income in the [Western Cape](313646.md) is the highest in South Africa. As of June 2025, the province's average household income is [R](298686.md)407,000. This amount is 35% higher than second-place [Gauteng](309661.md), and 50% above the national average.[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22]

### Employment levels

The professional, scientific, and technical services sector accounts for 23.34% (around 254,600 people) of the city's employment, which is the highest of any other industry.

The construction industry is the third-largest by number of employees, as it accounts for 9.29% (around 101,300 people) of the city's employment. The manufacturing sector, the second largest sector, also accounts for a large part of the city's employment opportunities as it provides 18.15% of the city's employment.[^cite_note-186]

Whereas unemployment has increased in numerous major cities across the country in recent years, in Cape Town, employment has increased. In 2025, it was reported that the city had diverged from a national trend, and added 86,000 jobs. The [Mayor of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Cape_Town) attributed this growth to good governance and [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure) investments, creating an attractive [business](39206.md) environment, and an increase in [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism).[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Deviates_from_National_Labour_Trends-23]

The Western Cape's unemployment levels are below the national average. Formal sector jobs in the Western Cape grew by 3.1% in 2024 - 61% higher than the national average.[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22]

### Economic output and growth

In 2008, Cape Town was considered the most [entrepreneurial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship) city in South Africa, with the percentage of Capetonians pursuing business opportunities almost three times higher than the national average. Capetonians aged between 18 and 64 were 190% more likely to pursue new business, whilst in Johannesburg, the same demographic group was only 60% more likely than the national average to pursue a new business.[^cite_note-187]

In 2019, the city's [GMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product) of R489 billion[^cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188] (US\$33.04 billion)[^cite_note-SARS_2019-189] represented 71.1% of the Western Cape's total [GRP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_regional_domestic_product) and 9.6% of South Africa's total GDP;[^cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188] the city also accounted for 11.1%[^cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188] of all employed people in the country and had a citywide [GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP_per_capita) of R111,364[^cite_note-Cape_Town_2020-188] (US\$7,524).[^cite_note-SARS_2019-189]



A commercial construction site in the suburb of [Tokai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokai,_Cape_Town)

As of 2024, Cape Town's labor force consists of 1.1 million workers. The largest sector in Cape Town is the professional and business services industry, which accounts for 29.61% of the city's workforce.

Cape Town is home to the headquarters of many of South Africa's largest companies, across a variety of industries, many of which are located in its [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD). As of 2022 data, 8 of South Africa's 50 [largest companies by annual revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_companies_in_South_Africa) were headquartered in Cape Town.

Cape Town has significantly above-average economic performance amongst its South African [metropolitan peers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)), and numerous companies have moved employees and their headquarters to the city in recent years.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_hot_spot_for_building_opportunities,_Gauteng_market_softening_–_WBHO-190] In 2025, according to [Statistics South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_South_Africa) (Stats SA), Cape Town had the lowest unemployment rate out of all South African cities.

### Major industries

Cape Town's economy consists of a diverse mix of industries. The [professional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Professional_services), scientific, and technical services sector forms a major part of the local economy. This sector includes a variety of fields, such as software development, management, accounting, consulting, architecture, design, engineering, legal, and RND.

#### Tech

Cape Town is recognized as a global tech hub, and is considered Africa's main tech hub. The city is home to the headquarters of many startups and established tech companies. Growing at an annual rate of 8.5%, the [tech](44211754.md) industry is a major part of Cape Town's economy.[^cite_note-IT_leads-191] Cape Town is home to numerous tech company headquarters, including those of Parket, Admyt, ParkUpp, Xneelo, [Jumo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumo), [Aerobotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerobotics), Cue, Vault22, [Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_\(telecommunications\)), and Luno.

[Fintech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fintech) is a particularly strong niche for Cape Town, and the city is home to over 60 fintech [startups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startups).[^cite_note-Invest_Cape_Town_-_Economy_&_Infrastructure-192] Major fintech companies with their headquarters in Cape Town include [Yoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoco), [Payfast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payfast), [SnapScan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SnapScan), [Zapper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zapper_\(company\)), [Peach Payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach_Payments), and [Mukuru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukuru_\(South_Africa\)).

The city also has HQs for a number of companies in the [space industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_industry).[^cite_note-193]

Cape Town is also home to the headquarters of fibre network operators (FNOs) [Frogfoot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frogfoot_Networks) and [Octotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octotel). The city also houses a regional office of FNO Liquid Intelligent Technologies. A number of entrepreneurial initiatives hosting tech [startups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup), such as the Sun Exchange, are located in the city.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-Cape_Town’s_Cue_raises_R82-million_to_take_AI_service_agents_global-195]

In terms of foreign tech companies, Cape Town houses the Africa HQ of ecommerce company [Amazon](90451.md), located at [Riverlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverlands_\(Cape_Town\)), as well as the second global hub of fintech [ClearScore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ClearScore).

#### Retail

Cape Town is a hub for South Africa's [retail industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retailing_in_South_Africa) as a whole, with the city housing the headquarters of nine of South Africa's major retail companies, including all but one of its largest [supermarket](51862.md) chains.

Major brick and mortar retailers headquartered in Cape Town include [Woolworths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolworths_\(South_Africa\)) (including [Absolute Pets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolute_Pets)); [Pick n Pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pick_n_Pay); [Shoprite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoprite_Holdings_Ltd) (including [Checkers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checkers_\(supermarket_chain\)) and [Petshop Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petshop_Science)); [Pepkor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepkor) (including [Pep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pep_\(South_Africa\)) and [Ackermans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ackermans)); [MRP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mr_Price_Group); [TFG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TFG_Limited) (including Foschini, Sportscene, TotalSports, and Bash); [Clicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clicks_\(South_Africa\)) (including [Sorbet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorbet_\(South_Africa\))); [Truworths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truworths); and [Cape Union Mart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Union_Mart).

South Africa's largest online retailer, [Takealot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takealot_Group) (including home delivery subsidiary Mr D) has its headquarters in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD).

Other, smaller retailers headquartered in Cape Town include [Yuppiechef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuppiechef), [Stodels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stodels), and Wootware.

#### Media

[Mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media) is another of Cape Town's major industries. The city is home to the headquarters of numerous of South Africa's largest print and digital news publications. Some of these are part of large mass media corporations, while others are independent, like [Daily Maverick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Maverick).

The headquarters of eMedia are located in Cape Town. eMedia operates South Africa's largest privately-owned [free-to-air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-to-air) [television](29831.md) station, [e.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.tv), as well as major digital news publication eNCA.



The [Media24 Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24_Centre) is the headquarters of [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers), South Africa's largest [mass media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media) company, and the country's [third-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_companies_in_South_Africa) listed company by annual revenue

Cape Town is also home to the headquarters of South Africa's largest mass media corporation, and the country's third-largest listed company by annual revenue, [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers). Located in the [Media24 Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24_Centre), Naspers owns numerous national publications through its subsidiary, [Media24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media24). These include [News24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News24_\(website\))), as well as its Afrikaans sister publication, Netwerk24.&#x20;

Other media companies headquartered in the city include business publication company Topco Media, and magazine publisher Highbury Safika Media. The city also has a regional office for [Primedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primedia).

#### Film

The city has the largest film industry in the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere)[^cite_note-196] generating R5 billion (US\$476.19 million) in revenue and providing an estimated 6,058 direct and 2,502 indirect jobs in 2013.[^cite_note-197] Much of the industry is based out of the [Cape Town Film Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Film_Studios).

#### Conferences



The [Cape Town International Convention Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre) (CTICC) hosts many local and international conferences and conventions

In recent years, Cape Town, has become a global [conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conference) hub, and a highly desirable place to host delegates for [meetings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meetings). The [Cape Town International Convention Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Convention_Centre) (CTICC) in Cape Town's [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) hosts many large conferences throughout each year. The city has been increasing in popularity in this regard, and in June 2025, it was reported that Cape Town had reached 35th place globally in the International Congress and Convention Association's (ICCA) rankings of popular destinations for international association meetings.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_climbs_global_rankings,_establishing_itself_as_a_premier_destination_for_international_meetings-198]

The report highlighted that Cape Town hosted 58 such meetings in 2024, which constitutes 60% of the total hosted across South Africa that year. Cape Town also placed among the top 10 cities worldwide for average attendance per event, with an average of 717 delegates per meeting.

The Western Cape's official tourism, trade, and investment promotion agency, Wesgro, stated that Cape Town and the Western Cape Convention Bureau secured 36 new conference bids in the 2024/25 financial year alone. These events are expected to generate [R](298686.md)745 million in economic impact, and attract over 27,000 delegates through to 2028.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_climbs_global_rankings,_establishing_itself_as_a_premier_destination_for_international_meetings-198]

In 2026, Cape Town was named *Africa's Leading Meetings & Conference Destination* at the World Travel Awards.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_earns_double_honours_at_travel_awards-20]

#### Renewable energy

The majority of South Africa's growing [renewable energy](25784.md) industry is based in the Western Cape.[^cite_note-199] Numerous domestic renewable energy companies are headquartered in Cape Town, including [Charge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charge_\(South_Africa\)), [Rubicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubicon_Group), Treetops, [SOLA Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOLA_Group), [Anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthem_\(energy_company\)), [Mulilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulilo), [Phelan Green Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phelan_Green_Group), [GreenCape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GreenCape), [Red Rocket Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Rocket_Energy), [NOA Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NOA_Group), African Clean Energy Developments, and G7 Renewable Energies. Foreign renewable companies with regional HQs in Cape Town include [ABO Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABO_Energy), [Acciona Energía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acciona_Energ%C3%ADa), [ACWA Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACWA_Power), [EDF Renewables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EDF_Renewables), Globeleq, JUWI, RES, and [Mainstream Renewable Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainstream_Renewable_Power).[^cite_note-Four_companies_pioneering_renewable_energy_in_Cape_Town-200]

All in all, 15 of SA's [23 major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_South_Africa#Energy_providers) renewable energy [independent power producers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_power_producers) (IPPs), amounting to 65%, have their headquarters in Cape Town.

#### Boat building

Cape Town has in recent years positioned itself as a notable player in the [boat building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boat_building) industry. The city has become a regional hub for the sector, and is home to numerous boat building yards. Robertson and Caine, one of the world’s largest producers of luxury [catamarans](195952.md), is headquartered in the city, as is high-tech racing yacht company Cape Racing Yachts, and superyacht specialist Southern Wind. Cape Town is home to a skilled boat building workforce and a dedicated group of artisans in the industry, and the city produces more luxury catamarans than any other city in the world.[^cite_note-Cape_Town’s_boatbuilding_sector_sails_to_global_success-201][^cite_note-US_tariffs_become_a_headwind_for_SA_yacht_builders-202]

#### Tourism



[Clifton Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town) is one of Cape Town's most famous beaches



[Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) from the [Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirstenbosch_National_Botanical_Garden)



[African penguins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_penguin) at [Boulders Penguin Colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulders_Beach)

The Western Cape is a highly important tourist region in South Africa; the tourism industry accounts for 9.8% of the GDP of the province and employs 9.6% of the province's workforce.[^cite_note-203]

In 2024, Cape Town had its busiest tourist season ever. Over 2.4 million tourists visited the city, spending around [R](298686.md)25 billion and supporting over 106,000 jobs (around 7% of the city's total employment). Cape Town is directly connected to 31 global destinations, with 226 international flights each week during its peak tourist season.[^cite_note-How_many_jobs_does_tourism_create_in_Cape_Town?-204]

Cape Town is not only a popular international tourist destination in [South Africa](17416221.md), but [Africa](5334607.md) as a whole. This is in part due to its mild climate, natural setting, costal geography, and well-developed infrastructure.

The city has several well-known natural features that attract tourists, most notably [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain),[^cite_note-205] which forms a large part of the [Table Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_National_Park) and is the back end of the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl,_Cape_Town). Reaching the top of the mountain can be achieved either by hiking up, or by taking the [Table Mountain Cableway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain_Cableway). [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point) is the dramatic headland at the end of the [Cape Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula).[^cite_note-206]

Many tourists also drive along [Chapman's Peak Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapman's_Peak), a narrow road that links [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town) with [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay,_Cape_Town), for the views of the Atlantic Ocean and nearby mountains. It is possible to either drive or hike up [Signal Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signal_Hill_\(Cape_Town\)) for closer views of the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), City Bowl area, and Table Mountain.[^cite_note-207]

Tourists also commonly visit [Cape Town's beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaches_of_Cape_Town), which are also very popular with local residents.[^cite_note-CTbeaches-208] It is possible to visit several different beaches in the same day, each with a different setting and atmosphere. Both coasts are popular, although the beaches in affluent [Clifton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town) and elsewhere on the Atlantic Coast are better developed, with restaurants and cafés lining Beach Road, alongside the various beaches.

The [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town), known as Cape Town's [Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riviera), is regarded as one of the most scenic routes in South Africa; along the slopes of the Twelve Apostles to the boulders and white sand beaches of [Llandudno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llandudno,_Cape_Town), with the route ending in [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay), a diverse suburb with a fishing and recreational boating harbour near a small island with a breeding colony of [African fur seals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_fur_seal).

Hout Bay is also accessible by road from the [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town) Valley, over the mountains to the northeast, and via the picturesque [Chapman's Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapman's_Peak) drive, from the residential suburb of [Noordhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noordhoek,_Cape_Town), situated in the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) area to the south-east.[^cite_note-209] [Boulders Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulders_Beach) near [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town) is known for its colony of [African penguins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_penguin).[^cite_note-210]

The city has a rich history, and several notable cultural attractions. Within [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) there are the [Artscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre) and [District Six Homecoming Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fugard) theaters, The Houses of [Parliament](376182.md), the Iziko museums and Planetarium, the South African National Gallery, the [Castle of Good Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castle_of_Good_Hope), and [The Company's Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company's_Garden) (South Africa's oldest park). The CBD is also home to many coffeehouses, as well as numerous restaurants that provide a variety of local cuisine options.

The [Victoria & Alfred Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_&_Alfred_Waterfront) is a mixed-use development built on top of part of the [docks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dock_\(maritime\)) of the [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town), and is the city's most-visited tourist attraction. It is also one of the city's most popular shopping venues, with several hundred shops as well as the [Two Oceans Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Oceans_Aquarium).[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-Sinclair_2011-212]

The V\&A also hosts the Nelson Mandela Gateway, through which [ferries](50771.md) depart for [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island).[^cite_note-213] It is possible to take a ferry from the V\&A to [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay,_Cape_Town), [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town,_Cape_Town) and the [Cape fur seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_fur_seal) colonies on Seal and Duiker Islands. Several companies offer tours of the [Cape Flats](251477.md), a region of mostly Coloured & Black [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Township_\(South_Africa\)).[^cite_note-214]

Within the metropolitan area, the most popular areas for visitors to stay include [Camps Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camps_Bay), [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point), the [V\&A Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_&_Alfred_Waterfront), the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), [Hout Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hout_Bay), [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town), [Rondebosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondebosch), [Newlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands,_Cape_Town), and [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West).[^cite_note-CTtourismstats-215] In November 2013, Cape Town was voted the best global city in *[The Daily Telegraph](23797577.md)'s* annual Travel Awards.[^cite_note-216]

Cape Town offers tourists a range of air-, land-, and sea-based adventure activities, including helicopter rides, [paragliding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paragliding) and [skydiving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skydiving), snorkelling and [scuba diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuba_diving), boat trips, game-fishing, hiking, mountain biking, and rock climbing. [Surfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surfing) is popular in the city, along both coasts, and Cape Town hosts the [Red Bull Big Wave Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Big_Wave_Africa) surfing competition every year. The city also has some local and international recreational scuba tourism.[^cite_note-217]

The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) works closely with [Cape Town Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Tourism) (CTT), the body tasked with promoting tourism in the city. Being the official destination [marketing](59252.md) agency for Cape Town, CTT has a mandate from the city to advocate for and promote tourism in the Cape Town metropolitan area, to both local and foreign markets.

The primary focus of Cape Town Tourism is to represent and advocate for Cape Town as an appealing tourist destination, as well as to track and report on metrics related to tourism in Cape Town (which CTT does as part of its Annual Report, published online).[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-Fin24-219] Cape Town Tourism receives a portion of its funding from the City of Cape Town while the remainder is made up of membership fees and own-generated funds.[^cite_note-220]

The [Tristan da Cunha](31361.md) government owns and operates a lodging facility in Cape Town which charges discounted rates to Tristan da Cunha residents and non-resident natives.[^cite_note-221] Cape Town's transport system links it to the rest of South Africa; it serves as the gateway to other destinations within the province. The [Cape Winelands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Winelands) and in particular the towns of [Stellenbosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch,_Western_Cape), [Paarl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paarl,_Western_Cape) and [Franschhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek,_Western_Cape) are popular day trips from the city for sightseeing and [wine tasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wine_tasting).[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223]

### Other

Other local industries include insurance, publishing, design, fashion, shipping, petrochemicals, architecture, advertising, legal, and real estate.[^cite_note-224]

Aside from fintech, numerous traditional [financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finance) firms are based in Cape Town, including [Coronation Fund Managers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_Fund_Managers), [Ninety One Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninety_One_Limited), [Old Mutual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Mutual), [Sanlam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanlam), [10X Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10X_Investments), [Santam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santam), and [Allan Gray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_Gray_\(company\)).

Two of SA's largest food manufacturers, [Pioneer Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Foods) (which includes [Bokomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bokomo) products) and Oceana Group, are headquartered in Cape Town. A further two – Sea Harvest and I\&J, have regional facilities in the city.

In terms of the oil and gas industry, Cape Town is home to the headquarters of [Engen Petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engen_Petroleum) and [Astron Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astron_Energy), South Africa's largest and second-largest [gas station](61260.md) operators by number of locations. The country's second-largest [oil refinery](195137.md), the [Astron Energy Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astron_Energy_Refinery), is also based in Cape Town.

Companies headquartered in the city that are not covered in the dedicated industry sections above include [Travelstart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travelstart), [Webafrica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Webafrica), [MWEB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MWEB), [LIFT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIFT_\(airline\)), [Pam Golding Properties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pam_Golding_Properties), [Levi Strauss South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Strauss_&_Co.), and [Adidas South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas). The [RE/MAX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RE/MAX) Southern Africa office is also in Cape Town.

The city is a manufacturing base for companies including [Hisense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hisense), [GlaxoSmithKline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GlaxoSmithKline), [Johnson & Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_&_Johnson), and [Kimberly-Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimberly-Clark).

### Inequality

The city of Cape Town's [Gini coefficient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gini_coefficient) of 0.58[^cite_note-225] is lower than South Africa's Gini coefficient of 0.7. This makes it more equal than the rest of the country, including any other major South African city, although still highly unequal by international standards.[^cite_note-IOL_inequality-226][^cite_note-Geni2000-227]

Between 2001 and 2010 the city's Gini coefficient, a measure of inequality, improved by dropping from 0.59 in 2007 to 0.57 in 2010[^cite_note-228] only to increase to 0.58 by 2017.[^cite_note-Socio-economic_profile-229]

## Housing



Modern, multi-story housing in [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West)

Cape Town has a mix of [detached](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detached_house) [homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home) of various sizes, [townhouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townhouses), and [apartments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartments). Certain [neighbourhoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighbourhoods) (especially older ones and those in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town)) tend to be dominated by larger, detached houses on separate plots of land, with parks and tree-lined streets. Newer suburbs, and those in the [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town), feature more of a mix of housing types, including low- and high-rise buildings and townhouse developments.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Property_Trends_and_Statistics-230]

### Home ownership

The city has for years had the most expensive [average home price in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_South_Africa). In September 2024, the average [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential) property price in Cape Town was [R](298686.md)2.23 million. This was an increase of 36% from the average price of R1.63 million in 2014 (a decade before), indicating consistently strong returns on [investment](63121.md) for those in the Cape Town property market. Sellers often get their asking price, or within 5% of it.[^cite_note-Airbnb_trend_hiking_property_sales_in_Cape_Town's_CBD-231][^cite_note-Dark_clouds_for_Cape_Town_property_and_its_residents-232]

As of 2021, South Africa's upmarket real estate segment covered homes worth more than R1.5 million. Most of the country's high-value [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential) properties are located in the Western Cape, of which Cape Town is the capital.[^cite_note-National_Housing_Market_Report,_2021-233]

Cape Town's property market grows at a rate far exceeding that of other major metropolitan areas in the country.[^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22] For example, Statistics South Africa (Stats SA) reported that between January 2010 and November 2022, Cape Town property prices increased at around double the rate of those in Johannesburg.[^cite_note-Stats_SA's_new_property_price_index:_Cape_Town_has_become_more_expensive-234] The city has been ranked as the world's second-strongest-performing real estate market.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_residential_property_market_ranked_world's_second_top-performing_market-235]

This performance improved further in more recent years. In June 2025, it was reported in South Africa's Residential Property Price Index (RPPI) that Cape Town's property values had not only increased by significantly more than the national average, but had also increased by almost 4 times as much as second-place Johannesburg's.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_continues_to_lead_national_house_price_growth-236]

To afford the average home in Cape Town, a salary of over R80,000 per month is required, which is significantly higher than in other provinces, and around 3 times the national average salary in South Africa.[^cite_note-56_jobs_that_pay_more_than_the_average_salary_of_R28,300_in_South_Africa-237] The [Western Cape](313646.md) province in general requires far higher salaries for affording to purchase the average property than in other provinces.[^cite_note-Here's_what_it_takes_to_afford_a_home_in_South_Africa-238]

Property in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town) offers a peaceful lifestyle and is generally more expensive, as is property in the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town) area, for its ocean views, and property in the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl) region, for its proximity to [Cape Town's CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) and amenities. Conversely, property in the [Cape Flats](251477.md) is cheaper on average, as the region is less desirable to live in. The same goes for property in the [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town) region, where lower costs are as a result of living further from central Cape Town, and more space to build outwards (both inland and up the west coast).

Some suburbs in Cape Town have had exceptionally high property value growth in recent years. For example, in 2025, it was reported that [Kalk Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalk_Bay), an old fishing village in the [False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay) area of Cape Town, had its house prices grow by around R1 million every year since 2020, due to an increase in demand for property in the Western Cape. The average price of a home in the suburb rose from R4.9 million in 2020 to R9 million at the start of 2025; an 83% increase over that 4-year period, or over 20% per year.[^cite_note-The_South_African_fishing_village_where_house_prices_increased_by_R1_million_every_year_since_2020-239][^cite_note-One_area_where_house_prices_increased_by_over_R4_million_in_5_years-240]

Likewise, the small town of [Scarborough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scarborough,_Western_Cape), which forms part of the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) and is popular with foreign home buyers, saw a 241% increase in property values over a 9-year period, with average home values rising from R2.30 million in 2016 to R7.85 million in 2025. One reason for this kind of housing price growth in Cape Town is the local municipality's investments in maintaining and upgrading neighbourhoods, to create an attractive environment for residents to live in.[^cite_note-The_South_African_fishing_village_where_house_prices_increased_by_R1_million_every_year_since_2020-239][^cite_note-One_area_where_house_prices_increased_by_over_R4_million_in_5_years-240][^cite_note-Foreigners_flocking_to_buy_property_in_this_tiny_seaside_village_in_South_Africa-241]

Property prices in Cape Town are around double the national average of R1.41 million, according to home loan statistics. In the fourth quarter of 2023, the national average home price recorded its second consecutive decrease. Meanwhile, Cape Town property prices have continued to increase.[^cite_note-What_the_average_house_price_in_South_Africa_gets_you_in_Joburg,_Durban_and_Cape_Town-242][^cite_note-Super_luxury_home-sales_boom,_Joburg_buyers_pay_more_for_Cape_Town_houses-243]

Cape Town's property values increase on average around 10% per year. That figure was 11.5% year-over-year in January 2026.[^cite_note-People_and_businesses_are_moving_to_where_things_just_work_in_South_Africa-244] The consistently strong property prices and growth thereof can be attributed to numerous factors, including good service delivery by [local government](4043753.md), a strong local economy, the coastal lifestyle that Capetonians enjoy, and semigration (individuals moving from other South African provinces into the Western Cape).[^cite_note-What_the_average_house_price_in_South_Africa_gets_you_in_Joburg,_Durban_and_Cape_Town-242][^cite_note-Super_luxury_home-sales_boom,_Joburg_buyers_pay_more_for_Cape_Town_houses-243][^cite_note-CT-26][^cite_note-South_Africans_flock_to_Cape_Town-22]

Cape Town trends among the top 20 cities worldwide for capital value growth, further increasing the attractiveness of owning a home in the city.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_foreign_buyer_hotspots_and_top_prices_paid_for_luxury_homes-245]

Cape Town housing is in short supply due to a number of issues, including [NIMBYISM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NIMBY), delays in approvals for social housing, a lack of space to build outwards, and an increase in properties listed as [short-term rentals](9590858.md) on [Airbnb](32058290.md) and similar platforms.[^cite_note-Dark_clouds_for_Cape_Town_property_and_its_residents-232][^cite_note-Cape_Town's_Southern_Suburbs:_Rising_prices_and_shorter_listing_times-246] The negative impact of the unchecked growth of short-term rentals, such as Airbnbs, on the housing market was confirmed in January 2026 by South African [Minister of Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Tourism_\(South_Africa\)) [Patricia de Lille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_de_Lille). She stated that the primary solution was to build more affordable housing close to [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD).[^cite_note-De_Lille_concedes_short-term_rentals_add_to_Cape_Town’s_housing_crisis-247]

In February 2026, the City of Cape Town announced plans to significantly increase [municipal rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rates_\(tax\)) for [short-term rental](9590858.md) properties, as part of combating low housing availability. The shift will result in short-term rentals, like those from [Airbnb](32058290.md) and [Booking.com](24842457.md), being charged commercial tariffs instead of residential ones. This would increase their tariffs by as much as 135%, and put them in the same category that [hotels](14276.md) and [guest houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guest_house) already are. As part of the announcement, the City stated it was correcting an imbalance in taxes, and that short-term rental operators should not be able to benefit from [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential) tariffs while running what is effectively a [commercial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commerce) business.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_cracks_down_on_Airbnb_rates-248]

Despite not being the [largest city in South Africa by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_South_Africa), Cape Town has built the highest number of affordable housing units, by a significant margin. During the period 1994 through 2025, the City of Cape Town constructed a total of 202,070 affordable housing units. In February 2026, it was reported that during the preceding five years, Cape Town had handed over a total of 12,401 affordable houses (equating to 2,480 per year). This was more affordable homes than the next two largest [metros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)) combined, and 152% more affordable homes than [Johannesburg](58893.md) (a significantly larger city by population) handed over during the same period.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_leads_metro_housing_race-249]

In early 2025, 14% of homes listed for sale were new to the market. The most common size of properties for sale was 2-bedroom, followed by 3-bedroom properties.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Property_Trends_and_Statistics-230]

The city is home to some of South Africa's most expensive neighbourhoods, including [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town) and [Bishopscourt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishopscourt,_Cape_Town) in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town); [Tamboerskloof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamboerskloof) and [Higgovale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higgovale,_Cape_Town) in the [City Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Bowl); and [Fresnaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresnaye,_Cape_Town), [Camps Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camps_Bay), [Clifton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town), [Llandudno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llandudno), and [Bantry Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantry_Bay,_Cape_Town) in the [Atlantic Seaboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Seaboard,_Cape_Town). Affluent areas are popular with international buyers from countries including the [United Kingdom](31717.md), the [Netherlands](21148.md), [Germany](11867.md), and [France](5843419.md), with some purchasing homes around R60 million (\~ £2.5 million / €3 million / US\$3.3 million).[^cite_note-Clifton_Remains_Cape_Town's_Most_Expensive_Suburb_to_Buy_Property-250][^cite_note-Super_luxury_home-sales_boom,_Joburg_buyers_pay_more_for_Cape_Town_houses-243][^cite_note-Cape_Town's_foreign_buyer_hotspots_and_top_prices_paid_for_luxury_homes-245] 9 out of South Africa's 10 most expensive suburbs are in Cape Town.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_tops_R21billion_in_transactions-251]

In 2021, Cape Town's [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential) property market was valued at R1.22 trillion, the highest out of all major South African [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)). The city's property market constituted 72% of the entire Western Cape's residential property market value, and 21% of the national property market value. The city had significantly more homes valued in the luxury market segment (at 43% of the total) than in any other major South African metro area. In the same year, there were around 800,000 residential properties located in Cape Town. 76% of those were detached homes, 15% were apartments, and 9% were semi-detached homes.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Housing_Market_Report_-_2021-252]

As of April 2025, Cape Town had a home ownership rate of 6.6% among those aged 18 to 35; 22.7% among those aged 36 to 49; 35.7% for those aged 50 to 64; and 35% for those aged 65 or older. In the same month, 25.8% of new home buyers in Cape Town were aged 18 to 35.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Property_Trends_and_Statistics-230]

### Rentals

In 2025, the average rent price in Cape Town for a 2-bedroom apartment was approximately [R](298686.md)12,000 per month. However, this value varies significantly between suburbs. For a property of the same size, some neighbourhoods offer rent as low as R7,000 per month, while in other, more desirable areas, this may be as high as R30,000 per month. The city has excellent gross rental yields, with some properties generating as much as a 10.5% return on investment for owners.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_Rental_Market_in_2025:_Key_Trends_&_Insights_for_Landlords_&_Tenants-253][^cite_note-Demand_for_SA_rental_property_on_the_rise-254]

Rental prices for apartments in affluent areas can often exceed the city's average by multiples. For example, in 2024, three areas in the Atlantic Seaboard – Camps Bay, Clifton, and Bantry Bay – averaged rental prices of R40,000 per month for a 1-bedroom apartment, and R120,000 per month for a 3-bedroom apartment. In Cape Town, popular rental website Property24 lists almost 100 rentals per month at prices over R100,000 per month, and some rentals cost as much as R300,000 per month.[^cite_note-These_Cape_Town_suburbs_charge_an_average_of_R120_000_for_rent-255]

Average rent prices in Cape Town increased by 5.2% in 2025, in line with the national average.[^cite_note-Fibonacci_development_set_to_attract_property_investors_and_to_aid_CPT_rental_shortage-256] As of 2024, the city has the lowest [rental vacancy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rental_vacancy_rate) out of all major South African metropolitan areas.[^cite_note-These_Cape_Town_suburbs_charge_an_average_of_R120_000_for_rent-255]

In 2021, approximately 18% of Cape Town residents rented their homes.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Housing_Market_Report_-_2021-252]

With nearly 5,000 students from over 100 countries studying at the [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town) (UCT), 18% of the student body is international, which increases demand for rental properties in Cape Town. Student housing – accommodation provided by the University of Cape Town – has a shortfall, which is made up by the private rental market. In 2023, there were 28,000 registered UCT students, with 8,400 of them securing UCT residence places.[^cite_note-Fibonacci_development_set_to_attract_property_investors_and_to_aid_CPT_rental_shortage-256]

As of 2025, rental prices in the Western Cape were the highest in South Africa, and 23% above the national average. It has been the most expensive province in South Africa for renting since 2016.[^cite_note-Dangerous_proposal_for_properties_in_Cape_Town-257][^cite_note-One_South_African_province_where_rental_prices_shot_up-258] The Western Cape's reputation as South Africa's best-run province, with efficient governance and high-quality, well-maintained infrastructure, has made it an attractive destination for tenants.[^cite_note-Fibonacci_development_set_to_attract_property_investors_and_to_aid_CPT_rental_shortage-256]

The average rent price in the Western Cape in the fourth quarter of 2024 was R11,141. This was the first time the figure had gone beyond the R11,000 mark. The Western Cape also has the lowest number of tenants in arrears (those who are behind on rental payments) out of all South African provinces.[^cite_note-New_record_for_the_average_rent_paid_in_South_Africa-259] The Western Cape had a record-low vacancy rate of 1.07% in the third quarter of 2024.[^cite_note-Fibonacci_development_set_to_attract_property_investors_and_to_aid_CPT_rental_shortage-256]

## Infrastructure and services

The City of Cape Town has a strong focus and high level of spending on [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_infrastructure) development and maintenance. The City spent a total of [R](298686.md)9.5 billion on infrastructure development during the 2024 to 2025 financial year.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_sets_all-time_record_for_infrastructure_spending-260] For the 2025 to 2026 financial year, the city's budget was R12.6 billion. Its total spend ended up being R12.24 billion – the highest single year infrastructure investment spend for a city in South Africa's history.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Mayor_announces_SA-record_infrastructure_investment-261] In the same year, the City apportioned R120 billion for its 10-year infrastructure portfolio.[^cite_note-2025_to_2026_Budget_-_Annexure_A-24] Cape Town has a larger infrastructure budget than the combined budgets of all three [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)) in the province of [Gauteng](309661.md).[^cite_note-2025_to_2026_Budget_-_Annexure_A-24]

Most goods are handled through the [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town) or [Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport). Most major shipbuilding companies have offices in Cape Town.[^cite_note-262] The province is also a centre of energy development for the country, with the existing [Koeberg nuclear power station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg_nuclear_power_station) providing energy for the Western Cape's needs.[^cite_note-263]

Cape Town has five major [commercial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commerce) nodes, with the city's [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) containing around 40% of its office space.[^cite_note-State_of_Cape_Town_Central_City_Report_2022-264] [Century City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Century_City_\(Cape_Town\)), the [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Western_Cape)/Tygervalley strip, Westlake, and [Claremont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claremont,_Cape_Town) commercial nodes are well-established and contain many offices and corporate [headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headquarters), for both local and international companies.

Cape Town's [property investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_investment) and [construction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction) markets consistently perform well above its metropolitan peers in South Africa.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_hot_spot_for_building_opportunities,_Gauteng_market_softening_–_WBHO-190]

In September 2025, the City implemented new planning rules for property development that reduce approval times. Through its Municipal Planning Amendment by-law, costs for developers, and by extension buyers, are reduced. Furthermore, opportunities opened up for small-scale developers to build up to 12 [affordable](3531662.md) rental units on certain residential plots, without the need to go through a [rezoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rezoning) process, thereby speeding up the building of [affordable housing in Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affordable_housing_in_South_Africa#Cape_Town). The City stated that it was also investigating the use of [artificial intelligence](1164.md) (AI) to process future development applications.[^cite_note-New_property_rules_for_Cape_Town_to_speed_up_planning_approvals-265]

### Telecommunications

Cape Town has a well-established telecoms infrastructure network, which includes:

- [Fibre to the premises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiber_to_the_x) for residential and commercial buildings. Fibre network operators (FNOs) like [Vumatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vumatel), Frogfoot, and [Octotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octotel), provide connectivity as high as 1 Gbit/s, with a separate 1 Gbit/s redundant line, to homes and business premises. These services also provide optional [VoIP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VoIP) connectivity.

  [^cite_note-Vumatel_beats_Openserve_in_close_fibre_race-266]

  - The city derives its main [undersea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undersea_cable) connectivity from [West African](67393.md) cable routes, such as WACS, Equiano, and SACS, as well as Pan African cables like 2africa. Many of these land at either [Yzerfontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yzerfontein), around 2 hours from Cape Town, or [Melkbosstrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkbosstrand), just to the north of the city.

    [^cite_note-All_the_undersea_cables_connecting_South_Africa_to_the_rest_of_the_world-267]

- [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) and [4G](486547.md) [cellular networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cellular_network), with coverage in almost all areas of the metro region, from major providers such as [Vodacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodacom), [MTN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Group), and [Cell C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cell_C), as well as options from [MVNOs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MVNO) like [Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_\(telecommunications\)).

  [^cite_note-Best_mobile_network_in_Cape_Town_revealed-268]

### Health

The city contains many well-equipped clinics and hospitals in both the public and private healthcare sectors.

See also: [List of hospitals in South Africa § Cape Metropole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hospitals_in_South_Africa#Cape_Metropole)



[Groote Schuur Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groote_Schuur_Hospital)

- The [Alexandra Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandra_Hospital_\(Cape_Town\)) is a specialist mental health care hospital in Cape Town. It provides care for complex mental health issues and intellectual disability.

  [^cite_note-269]

- [Groote Schuur Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groote_Schuur_Hospital) is a large, government-funded, teaching hospital situated on the slopes of Devil's Peak. It was founded in 1938 and is famous for being the institution where the first human-to-human heart transplant took place. Groote Schuur is the chief academic hospital of the University of Cape Town's medical school, providing tertiary care and instruction in all the major branches of medicine. The hospital underwent major extension in 1984 when two new wings were added.

- Helderberg Hospital is a district hospital for the Helderberg basin. Located in Somerset West, it also serves surrounding areas in the Overberg district.

Numerous of South Africa's major private medical services groups operate [hospitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_hospital) in the city, including those below.

- [Mediclinic Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediclinic_Group): 7 facilities (Cape Town, Milnerton, Louis Leipoldt, Constantiaberg, Cape Gate, Durbanville, and Vergelegen)

  [^cite_note-Hospitals_&_Day_Clinics-270]

- [Netcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netcare): 4 facilities (Christiaan Barnard, UCT Private Academic, Blaawberg, and N1 City)

  [^cite_note-Netcare_Smart_Search-271]

- [Melomed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melomed): 5 facilities (Gatesville, Bellville, Tokai, Mitchells Plain, and Claremont)

  [^cite_note-Melomed-272]

- [Life Healthcare Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_Healthcare_Group): 3 facilities (Kingsbury, Vincent Pallotti, and Sports Science)

  [^cite_note-Life_Healthcare_-_Hospitals_in_Cape_Town-273]

### Education



The [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town)'s Upper Campus, in front of [Devil's Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil's_Peak_\(Cape_Town\))



The Cape Town Campus of the [Cape Peninsula University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula_University_of_Technology), with [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) in the background

Public primary and secondary schools in Cape Town are run by the [Western Cape Education Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Education_Department). This provincial department is divided into seven districts; four of these are "Metropole" districts – Metropole Central, North, South, and East – which cover various areas of the metropolis.[^cite_note-274] There are also many private schools, both religious and secular. Cape Town has a well-developed higher system of [public universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_university).

Cape Town is served by three public universities: the [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town) (UCT), the [University of the Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Western_Cape) (UWC) and the [Cape Peninsula University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Peninsula_University_of_Technology) (CPUT). [Stellenbosch University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch_University), while not based in the metropolitan area itself, has its main campus and administrative section 50 kilometers from Cape Town, and has additional campuses, such as the Tygerberg Faculty of Medicine and Health Sciences and the Bellville Business Park, north-west of the city in the town of [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_South_Africa).

Both the University of Cape Town and Stellenbosch University are leading universities in South Africa. This is due in large part to substantial financial contributions made to these institutions by both the public and private sector.

UCT is an [English language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) tuition institution. It has over 21,000 students and has an MBA program that was ranked 51st by the Financial Times in 2006.[^cite_note-275]

It is also the top-ranked university in Africa, being the only African university to make the world's Top 200 university list at number 146.[^cite_note-276] UCT has its main campus on the mountainside in [Rondebosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondebosch), a predominantly residential suburb in the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town) region of Cape Town. The university also operates satellite campuses in [Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observatory,_Cape_Town) and [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD).

Since the [African National Congress](2503.md) has become the country's ruling party, some restructuring of Western Cape universities has taken place and as such, traditionally non-white universities have seen increased financing, which has evidently benefitted UWC.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]

The Cape Peninsula University of Technology was formed on 1 January 2005, when two separate institutions – [Cape Technikon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Technikon) and [Peninsula Technikon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsula_Technikon) – were merged. The new university offers education primarily in English, although one may take courses in any of South Africa's official languages. The institution generally awards the [National Diploma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Diploma_\(South_Africa\)).

Students from the universities and high schools are involved in the South African [SEDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEDS), Students for the Exploration and Development of Space. This is the South African SEDS, and there are many SEDS branches in other countries, preparing enthusiastic students and young professionals for the growing Space industry.[^cite_note-279]

As well as the universities, there are also several [colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College) in and around Cape Town. Including the [College of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Cape_Town), False Bay College and Northlink College. Many students use NSFAS funding to help pay for tertiary education at these TVET colleges.[^cite_note-280] Cape Town has also become a popular [study abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Study_abroad_in_the_United_States) destination for many international college students. Many [study abroad providers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Study_abroad_organization) offer semester, summer, short-term, and internship programs in partnership with Cape Town universities as a chance for international students to gain intercultural understanding.

### Safety and security

Cape Town has multiple [law enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement) agencies working together to ensure the [laws of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_South_Africa), as well as the provincial and municipal [bylaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bylaws), are upheld throughout the city. Different agencies have different jurisdictions, granted to them by various levels of government. In recent years, the City of Cape Town and [Western Cape](313646.md) province have been pushing to gain more independence from the [South African Police Service](1228194.md), and to instead have full control of their [policing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Policing).[^cite_note-WC_crime_statistics_prompt_calls_for_devolution_of_policing_powers-281][^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_pushes_for_control_over_police_investigations-282]

In recent years, the city has taken a focused approach towards combatting crime, investing in technological interventions such as dash and body cameras, CCTV, and drones.[^cite_note-City_boosts_its_tech_investment_for_safety-283] The Western Cape's Department of Police Oversight and Community Safety had an annual budget in 2024 of around R800 million, a large portion of which goes to Cape Town. Likewise, the City of Cape Town's Safety and Security Directorate has a healthy annual budget, which totaled around half a billion rand for the 2024 fiscal year.[^cite_note-City_boosts_its_tech_investment_for_safety-283][^cite_note-MEC_Reagen_Allen_tables_Western_Cape_Police_Oversight_and_Community_Safety_budget_2024-284]

Cape Town's law enforcement is overseen by the city's Mayco Member for Safety and Security, the Western Cape Community Safety and Police Oversight [MEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_\(South_Africa\)), and the Western Cape Police Commissioner.[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_welcomes_113_new_metro_and_traffic_officers-285][^cite_note-Law_enforcement_fighting_tooth_and_nail_to_rid_Cape_Town_of_crime_stigma-286]

The agencies working to ensure safety and security in the City of Cape Town are:

#### The Cape Town Metropolitan Police Department (Metro Police)

See also: [Municipal Police (South Africa)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_Police_\(South_Africa\))



Cape Town Metro Police officers and a Metro Police vehicle

The Metro Police are responsible for crime prevention, as well as the enforcement of municipal bylaws and traffic laws, conducting autonomous drug- and alcohol-related operations, executing warrants in support of the [South African Police Service](1228194.md), and conducting visible policing in specific areas. Its special units include:

- Gang and Drug Task Team

- Tactical Response Unit

- Canine Unit

- Equestrian Unit

- Camera Response Unit

- Video Unit

- CCTV Unit

- Social Crime Prevention Unit

  [^cite_note-Metropolitan_Police_Services_Department-287]

In mid-2025, Cape Town rolled out its Neighbourhood Safety Officer (NSO) program. Part of the Metro Police, NSOs are officers dedicated to patrolling specific neighbourhoods across the city. On top of other kinds of law enforcement, the program positions 5 dedicated NSOs per City of Cape Town municipal ward.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_mayor_shows_off_700_new_officers_who_will_be_hitting_the_streets_to_keep_the_city_safe-288]

The Metro Police make use of vehicles including the [Toyota Corolla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Corolla) for general law enforcement, the [Volkswagen Golf GTI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Golf) for the Flying Squad, and the [Toyota Fortuner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Fortuner) for the Highway Patrol.

#### Traffic Service



Cape Town Traffic Service officer on a motorbike in the [V\&A Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V\&A_Waterfront)

The city's Traffic Service has dedicated officers for improving road safety by providing effective [driving license](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driving_license) services and [traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic) enforcement. The Service employs specially trained peace officers.

While Metro Police can and do also provide traffic-related enforcement, the Traffic Service focuses entirely on such. Cape Town's Traffic Service enforces traffic laws, ensures [roadworthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roadworthy) compliance of all [motor vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_vehicles), and provides driving license services. Matters it deals with include [drunk driving](29981150.md) offenses, vehicle impounds, animal impounds, displaced people, [graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) enforcement, informal trading, [liquor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liquor) enforcement, metal theft, [rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway) enforcement, and neighbourhood safety.[^cite_note-Law_Enforcement,_Traffic_and_Coordination_Department-289]

Traffic Services began using dashboard-mounted cameras (dashcams) in 2023, for added security. Cape Town's Traffic Services operates a specialized Highway Patrol unit, with its own fleet of vehicles that are geared towards maintaining safety on the highways passing through the city.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_Law_Enforcement_Becomes_SA's_First_to_Use_Camera_Tech-290]

#### Law Enforcement Advancement Plan (LEAP)

LEAP officers are specifically deployed in high-crime areas. They undergo separate training, and exist as an entirely separate police force to Metro Police. However, they work closely with Metro Police, as well as SAPS.

LEAP officers deal with matters including the confiscation of illegal [firearms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firearms), violence prevention, [drug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug) and liquor confiscations, and anti-[gang](80408.md) activities.[^cite_note-Law_Enforcement_Advancement_Plan_(LEAP)-291]

The City of Cape Town partnered with the Western Cape Government to launch LEAP in 2020, as part of the latter's Western Cape Safety Plan (WCSP), which was launched in September 2019.[^cite_note-MEC_Reagen_Allen_on_training_of_Law_Enforcement_Advancement_Plan_(LEAP)_officers-292]

#### The South African Police Service (SAPS)



SAPS vehicles outside the main entrance to [Parliament](376182.md), in the center of Cape Town CBD

[SAPS](1228194.md) is the national policing authority in [South Africa](17416221.md). As such, they have a presence in Cape Town, as with all other major [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)). Their jurisdiction covers the investigation of crimes (which may be handed over to them by the Metro Police). SAPS employs detectives, criminologists, forensic pathologists, and other related experts, as is governed by the [Department of Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Police_\(South_Africa\)) in the national government.[^cite_note-SAPS_-_Home-293]

SAPS, along with Metro Police members, are held accountable by the [Independent Police Investigative Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Police_Investigative_Directorate), which investigates wrongdoing within the police service across South Africa.

In the City of Cape Town, SAPS makes use of vehicles including the [Toyota Hilux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Hilux), [Toyota Fortuner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Fortuner), and [Nissan Navara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nissan_Navara).

In early 2025, the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) announced its intention to merge numerous police forces info a single law enforcement agency.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_to_merge_traffic,_law_enforcement_and_metro_police_into_single_force-294]

#### Improvement Districts



A CCID trailer, guard and manager in the city centre providing additional security to the local area.

[^cite_note-CCID_-_Safety_and_Security-295]

Ratepayers in Cape Town fund a number [Business Improvement Districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_improvement_district) (BID) across the city with the assistance of the City of Cape Town. As of 2025 there were 21 BIDs[^cite_note-296] funded by local ratepayers across the city to provide localised supplementary urban management, communications, and social development services in their respective areas.[^cite_note-\:03-297]

The largest and oldest such district is the Cape Town [Central City Improvement District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CCID) (CCID), founded in 2000. CCID focuses on a roughly 1.6 sqkm area in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), the city's main economic hub.[^cite_note-About_the_CCID-298] The CCID also employs 12 dedicated Law Enforcement officers to assist with arrests. It is able to obtain access to the City of Cape Town's Strategic Surveillance Unit (SSU) CCTV cameras, to assist with crime prevention efforts.[^cite_note-CCID_-_Safety_and_Security-295]

The presence of BIDs like the CCID has been credited with significantly contributing to the general safety and prosperity of the city.[^cite_note-CCID_-_Safety_and_Security-295][^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301]

#### Law Enforcement Auxiliary Service (LEAS)

Cape Town's Law Enforcement Auxiliary Service (LEAS) has been operating since 2013, as part of the city's overall public safety program. LEAS comprises around 300 individuals, who volunteer for a minimum of 16 hours per month. LEAS volunteers operate under the supervision of permanent law enforcement officers. Uniformed LEAS officers assist with duties including patrolling (visible policing), enforcing municipal bylaws, and directing traffic. Non-uniformed officers assist with administrative tasks relating to law enforcement.[^cite_note-Volunteer_with_the_Law_Enforcement_Auxiliary_Service-302]

LEAS officers undergo thorough vetting, are divided up between the 4 geographical areas within the metro, and are supported by an Auxiliary Animal Control Unit, Marine Unit, and Rural Safety Unit.[^cite_note-Volunteers_dedicate_nearly_70_000_hours_towards_a_safer_city-303]

#### Other security measures

The City of Cape Town's Strategic Surveillance Unit (SSU), uses a system of around 2,000 [closed circuit television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed_circuit_television) (CCTV) [cameras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameras)[^cite_note-304] throughout the metropolitan area, monitoring suburbs for any suspicious activity, and using CCTV footage in the prosecution of crimes. The CCTV network is used by, among other agencies, Metro Police, LEAP, and SAPS (including for investigations). [Control room](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_room) staff for the CCTV network are able to rapidly deploy response teams, ranging from police to emergency services, based on the events occurring. The cameras are therefore useful not only for crime prevention, but also general [public safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_safety).[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-City_boosts_its_tech_investment_for_safety-283]

CCTV camera budgeting falls under the allocations of the Safety and Security Directorate, which also deploys communications equipment for various Cape Town neighbourhood watch organizations. The city's CCTV Cameras are also used for license plate recognition, by means of Automated Number Plate Recognition (ANPR) software. This is used by units including the city's Highway Patrol. In 2019, the City spent around R20 million on upgrading this system and purchasing new security vehicles.[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_to_add_dozens_of_CCTV_cameras_to_surveillance_network-306][^cite_note-City_boosts_its_tech_investment_for_safety-283]

Since 2024, Cape Town has also made use of its Eye in the Sky (Eye) Information, Surveillance, and Reconnaissance initiative – an advanced, [drone](58900.md)-based aerial technology system designed to provide real-time monitoring of activities on the ground. Forming part of a broader push to integrate surveillance technologies into public safety and urban governance, Eye's unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) have been specially designed for fighting crime, and are considerably advanced as a result.[^cite_note-Watching_from_above:_Surveillance_in_Cape_Town-307]

The drones are equipped with infrared cameras, and can detect body heat in cold water, trace residual heat from discharged firearms, and capture the thermal signatures of high-speed vehicles. Furthermore, the drones can fly at very high altitudes, operate for long periods of time, and function in adverse weather conditions.[^cite_note-Watching_from_above:_Surveillance_in_Cape_Town-307]

The City uses similar drones for environmental issues, such as mountain fires and illegal marine poaching. Cape Town's Eye drones deliver high-resolution, real-time intelligence to the city's security agencies and emergency response teams, forming an integral part of the city's overall safety and security operations.

Other forms of technology added recently to the city's safety and security arsenal include dashboard-mounted Automated Number Plate Recognition cameras, bodycams for police officers, gunshot detection systems for remote alerts, and handheld digital devices which are linked to the city's Emergency Policing Incident Control (EPIC) system, for fast-tracked incident logging, issuing of fines, and uploading of photographic evidence. Cape Town continues to modernize its policing systems, and in recent years, has spent over R600 million on technological advancements in the security space.[^cite_note-City_boosts_its_tech_investment_for_safety-283]

Many Cape Town residents have access to additional security in the form of private security subscriptions, from the likes of large companies (with national reach), such as [Fidelity ADT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidelity_ADT). These companies provide [armed](11966.md) response teams for [residential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential) crime reduction, with private security personnel and vehicles also being stationed around Cape Town neighbourhoods for visible, localized security. ADT also partners with major South African fibre network operator (FNO) [Vumatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vumatel), to monitor areas via CCTV, and some suburbs' residents have arranged for cameras to be installed around their houses for 24/7 surveillance, to increase local security.



Private security vehicles in [Meadowridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadowridge,_Cape_Town), Cape Town

The city is part of the Public Emergency Call Center (107) network, for crime reporting and response.[^cite_note-Alderman_JP_Smith:_Mayoral_Committee_Member_for_Safety_and_Security-308]

Cape Town has been a pioneer with policing and security innovations in South Africa. The City runs the country's only police reservist service that is managed by a local government, in the form of the Disaster Volunteers, and the Law Enforcement Auxiliary Service. It was also first to launch specialized policing units including the Gang and Drug Task Team, and the Metal Theft Unit.[^cite_note-Alderman_JP_Smith:_Mayoral_Committee_Member_for_Safety_and_Security-308]

The city is also the only metropolitan area in South Africa to use gunfire detection systems in high-risk areas, and to effectively implement computer-aided dispatch and integrated spatially enabled resource management through its award-winning EPIC system. Furthermore, Cape Town employs the largest CCTV network out of all South African cities.[^cite_note-Alderman_JP_Smith:_Mayoral_Committee_Member_for_Safety_and_Security-308]

### Energy supply



The [Steenbras Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steenbras_Power_Station), which is used to generate hydroelectric power for the city

As with many other forms of infrastructure, Cape Town stands out amongst South Africa's metropolitan areas for having a far more developed, stable [energy supply](9540.md). The city is home to the country's only nuclear power station - [Koeberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg_Nuclear_Power_Station) – as well as numerous other forms of power generating plants. A second nuclear station, situated in Duynefontein (slightly south-east of Koeberg) is planned. The new plant received Environmental Authorization in 2025, and awaits further statutory approvals by the national government.[^cite_note-Government_upholds_Eskom's_approval_to_build_new_nuclear_power_station-309]

Furthermore, Cape Town uses a system of pumped hydro electricity storage (at [Steenbras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steenbras_Power_Station)) to supplement its own power generation and avoid up to 2 stages of [load shedding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_shedding) (power outages), when the rest of the national grid goes down due to [insufficient energy to meet demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_energy_crisis). This results in not only residential homes having more sustained power feeds, but also allows commercial and industrial operations to continue when other major areas in the country are without power, boosting Cape Town's economic output.

#### Energy infrastructure developments

In January 2024, it was reported that the City of Cape Town intended to leave national energy operator [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom) behind, and become completely energy self-sustaining. Through adding 650MW of independent power to the city's grid, Cape Town aims to protect itself against the first 4 stages of Eskom load-shedding by 2026, with the goal of eventually becoming entirely self-sufficient, meeting its own electricity demand. The closing date for tender submissions for this project was 8 April 2024.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_plan_to_leave_Eskom_behind-310]

The City aims to procure power from existing generators, and will follow a two-pronged structure, allowing bidders to supplement dispatchable or reserve power with an optional self-dispatchable component – the latter being at a lower cost than the prevailing Eskom Megaflex Tariff.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_plan_to_leave_Eskom_behind-310]

Cape Town was able to go ahead with this plan after successful advocacy to the national government for the integration of independent power producers (IPPs) into the national grid. The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) took the Department of Mineral Resources and Energy (DMRE) and the National Energy Regulator of South Africa (Nersa) to court, after Nersa said it could not allow for independent power production unless the Ministry made changes.[^cite_note-ER-311]

Cape Town's progress with a holistic energy plan, detached from Eskom, was achieved following the gazetting of amendments to the electricity regulations for new electricity generation capacity in 2020, by then-Mineral Resources and Energy Minister Gwede Mantashe.[^cite_note-ER-311]

The new regulations opened the way for municipalities in good financial standing to develop their own power generation projects. President Ramaphosa announced the changes in a speech to Parliament in the same year, which marked the first time municipalities were allowed to buy energy from sources other than state-owned Eskom's monopolistic system.[^cite_note-ER-311]

Recent and in-progress power diversification methods include:[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_plan_to_leave_Eskom_behind-310]

- Private Small-Scale Embedded Generation (SSEG), providing up to 100 MW

- Embedded independent power producer [renewable energy](25784.md), providing up to 200MW

- A dispatchable IPP Program, providing up to 500MW

- [Wheeling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheeling_\(electric_power_transmission\)), providing up to 350MW

- City-owned SSEG, providing up to 20MW

The City of Cape Town announced a "Cash for Power" program in 2024, as part of its overall Energy Strategy. The program allows for Cape Town households to earn money for selling excess power they generate via solar panels back to the city's grid – a first for South Africa. The City opened applications for residential households to feed power into the grid by means of grid-tied solar photovoltaic (PV) systems and bi-directional meters.[^cite_note-CoCT_launches_Cash_for_Power_programme_alongside_Energy_Strategy_unveiling-312]

During the announcement speech, [Cape Town's Mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Cape_Town) also criticized the [national government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_South_Africa) and [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom) for not fixing the [load shedding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_shedding) problems in South Africa, and said that Cape Town would not wait for matters with the national grid to be fixed, with the [City](4043753.md) instead making its own power generation plans.[^cite_note-CoCT_launches_Cash_for_Power_programme_alongside_Energy_Strategy_unveiling-312]

Cape Town has South Africa's most advanced plans to buy power on the open market. In February 2025, the city announced that its Cash for Power program had 1,842 active participants, and had paid out over R55 million to its residents and businesses as part of the program, via credits applied to their municipal energy bills. 1,090 small-scale generators (sellers) were residential customers, and 752 were commercial or industrial. The city also stated that it was on track to double the payouts in the program's second year of operation.[^cite_note-Cash_for_Power:_Capetonians_reach_R50m_earnings_mark-313]

The City further stated that it had launched an online system for registering solar PV systems for the program, and announced unveiled a cheaper, bi-directional meter to feed power back into the grid. Customers can offset their electricity bills without registering for the Cash for Power program.
