However, registering allows them to go into a credit status, with the City paying them back for any excess power generation.[^cite_note-Cash_for_Power:_Capetonians_reach_R50m_earnings_mark-313]

In 2025, the conclusion of a year-long electricity wheeling pilot project was announced. 562,800 kWh of renewable electricity was successfully wheeled during the pilot. Enpower Trading, Etana Energy, and [Equites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equites_\(company\)) were the traders for the process. Cape Town Mayor [Geordin Hill-Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geordin_Hill-Lewis) announced that the city would enter the next phase of the project, whereby it would promote the scaling up of power trading across Cape Town's electricity grid, based on bilateral and multilateral trading agreements.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_moves_to_open_electricity_grid_to_traders_after_pilot-314]

The Mayor described the milestone as an exciting leap forward, to diversify Cape Town's electricity supplier base beyond Eskom, and towards a future of decentralized electricity trading in South Africa.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_moves_to_open_electricity_grid_to_traders_after_pilot-314]

In June 2025, the City of Cape Town's Atlantis solar PV plant had finished the installation of around 2,400 solar panels out of a planned 12,850 panels, as part of a R200 million renewable-energy project. City of Cape Town Energy MEC Xanthea Limberg stated that the Atlantis site would operate as a hybrid plant, alongside the city's first utility-scale battery storage operation, totaling 8 MW. There are plans for a 60 MW solar plant to be built at Paardevlei, which is expected to provide sufficient protection from one stage of Eskom load shedding.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_own-build_solar_plant_on_track_for_year-end_completion-315]

Cape Town has a goal of net-zero carbon municipal buildings by 2030, as well as a goal for alternative energy to meet 35% of the city's total demand by 2030.[^cite_note-Cape_Town's_own-build_solar_plant_on_track_for_year-end_completion-315]

The city's changes to its power infrastructure represent major changes in South Africa's overall electricity network, including a shift towards green energy, the integration of wheeling processes, a move towards energy diversification as a reaction to rising Eskom tariffs, and shorter-term, flexible contracts in a merchant market providing businesses with easier methods to source stable power.[^cite_note-Beyond_load_shedding:_4_key_energy_trends_reshaping_SA_in_2025-316]

In February 2026, [Cape Town Mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Mayor) [Geordin Hill-Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geordin_Hill-Lewis) announced that the [City](4043753.md) would that same month begin the process of engaging with a private electricity trading company to provide Cape Town with additional energy. The agreement would significantly shift how the city sources its power, breaking the monopoly long-held by national producer Eskom, and allowing Cape Town to appoint a trading company to purchase power from a range of private energy providers.

The City has already entered into numerous supply agreements with various companies that would, following the appointment of the trading company, build the required generation capacity. The Mayor said that using a trading platform alleviates the need to negotiate private power agreements contract by contract directly with providers, streamlining the energy sourcing process for the City.

Energy sourced via the new trading method and private providers is set to be cheaper than that sourced from Eskom, and will also mostly be derived from [renewable](25784.md) sources, in keeping with Cape Town's overarching net-zero carbon emission goals.[^cite_note-Major_South_African_city_dumping_Eskom-317]

In September 2026, Cape Town became the first city in South Africa to sign a [power purchase agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_purchase_agreement) with an [independent power producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_power_producer) (IPP), allowing the municipality to source energy outside of [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom), and at competitive rates. This was possible due to the recent [liberalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalization) of South Africa's [energy market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_South_Africa), which was historically controlled exclusively by [state-owned enterprise](204768.md) Eskom.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_seals_first_agreements_to_buy_power_at_below_Eskom_rates-318]

The [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) signed two [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_energy) purchasing agreements, worth a combined 70MW, and has plans to procure a further 130MW, for a total of 200MW under the current tendering process. The City said this would prevent unexpected price hikes to consumers, as the private energy increases would be linked to the [Consumer Price Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index_in_South_Africa) (CPI) each year. The energy procured from the new IPPs came in at around 20% cheaper than Eskom purchasing rates.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_seals_first_agreements_to_buy_power_at_below_Eskom_rates-318]

### Water supply

Main article: [Western Cape Water Supply System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape_Water_Supply_System)

Cape Town's water is supplied via the **Western Cape Water Supply System** (WCWSS), a complex water supply system in the [Western Cape](313646.md) province. The system comprises an interlinked network of six main dams, as well as pipelines, tunnels, distribution networks, and a number of minor dams - some owned and operated by the national [Department of Water and Sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Water_and_Sanitation), and some by the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md) [metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_\(South_Africa\)) government.[^cite_note-Kasrils-319]

Cape Town has extremely high-quality drinking water, ranking the best in South Africa as of February 2026, according to [Stats SA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stats_SA).[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_Water_Supply_-_Stats_SA-320]

#### Water crisis of 2017 to 2018

Main article: [Cape Town water crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_water_crisis)



Water crisis warning in Cape Town in 2018

The Cape Town water crisis of 2017 to 2018 was a period of severe water shortage in the [Western Cape](313646.md) region, most notably affecting the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md). While dam water levels had been declining since 2015, the Cape Town water crisis peaked during mid-2017 to mid-2018 when water levels hovered between 15 and 30 percent of total dam capacity.

In late 2017, there were first mentions of plans for "Day Zero", a shorthand reference for the day when the water level of the major dams supplying the city could fall below 13.5 percent.[^cite_note-USA_today-321][^cite_note-Globe_and_Mail-322][^cite_note-Poplak_2018-323] "Day Zero" would mark the start of Level 7 water restrictions, when municipal water supplies would be largely switched off and it was envisioned that residents could have to queue for their daily ration of water. If this had occurred, it would have made the City of Cape Town the first major city in the world to run out of water.[^cite_note-Booysen_et_al_2019-324][^cite_note-Booysen_et_al_2019a-325]

The city of Cape Town implemented significant water restrictions in a bid to curb water usage, and succeeded in reducing its daily water usage by more than half to around 500 million litres (130,000,000 US gal) per day in March 2018.[^cite_note-Narrandes_2018-326] The fall in water usage led the city to postpone its estimate for "Day Zero", and strong rains starting in June 2018 led to dam levels recovering.[^cite_note-Myburgh_2018-327]

In September 2018, with dam levels close to 70 percent, the city began easing water restrictions, indicating that the worst of the water crisis was over.[^cite_note-reduce-sep2018-328] Good rains in 2020 effectively broke the drought and resulting water shortage when dam levels reached 95 percent.[^cite_note-After_drought-329] Concerns have been raised, however, that unsustainble demand and limited water supply could result in future drought events.[^cite_note-330]

#### Water infrastructure developments

The City of Cape Town has mapped out a New Water Program, and Water Strategy, which plan to add 300 million litres of water per day to the city's supply, through measures including desalination, tapping into groundwater, and clearing invasive species.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_to_wrap_up_desalination_plant_feasibility_study_in_2025,_first_water_possible_in_2030-331]

In mid-2024, the Cape Town municipality stated that it was embarking on an infrastructure stability program, which involves refurbishing infrastructure and building new capacity, with funds for the program already having been added to the city's budget.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_needs_to_spend_well_over_R2_billion_to_achieve_water_security_in_the_next_decade-332]

The City of Cape Town announced in 2024 that it planned to build a desalination plant, to further strengthen Cape Town's water supply. A feasibility study was planned to be completed in 2025. The facility is expected to produce between 50 million and 70 million litres of water a day, with a targeted starting date of 2030.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_to_wrap_up_desalination_plant_feasibility_study_in_2025,_first_water_possible_in_2030-331]

The plant will be located on undeveloped land, at the intersection of the R27 and N1, in the vicinity of the [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town). The city is undertaking an environmental impact assessment process for the project.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_needs_to_spend_well_over_R2_billion_to_achieve_water_security_in_the_next_decade-332]

City of Cape Town Water and Sanitation MMC Zahid Badroodien stated that the proposed Paarden Island desalination plant would feature a 22,000 square meter sea water, reverse-osmosis operation, for use by Cape Town households. The estimated capital cost of the plant was roughly R5 billion, excluding VAT, and will be funded through the city's water tariff.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_to_wrap_up_desalination_plant_feasibility_study_in_2025,_first_water_possible_in_2030-331]

### Future infrastructure developments

As part of Cape Town's Vision 2050 project, the City aims to significantly upgrade its infrastructure. The 25-year project, unveiled by the City in mid-2025, was opened for public comment for a period of around 2 months. At the time, Cape Town's Mayor stated that the project's overarching goal was for Cape Town to remain a, "future-fit city of opportunities for all". 75% of the project's goals will directly benefit lower-income households.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_unveils_ambitious_25-year_vision-333]

Some of Vision 2050's aims include:[^cite_note-Cape_Town_unveils_ambitious_25-year_vision-333][^cite_note-Cape_Town's_Vision_2050:_Mayor_calls_for_comment_on_the_City's_Long-Term_Plan-334]

- Public transport accounting for 75% of all trips, with no resident spending more than 10% of their income on commuting.

- 35% of electricity obtained from alternative sources by 2030, supported by grid upgrades.

- 25% of water obtained from non-surface sources by 2040.

- 50,000 new housing opportunities created annually, through both land releases and private-sector partnerships.

- Taking over passenger rail from the national government, to revive it as the city's transport backbone.

- Expanding the city's policing powers to combat crime more effectively. This includes Smart Policing that is data and tech-driven.

- Diverting 70% of waste from landfills through methods including recycling and better waste management.

- Increased use of digital technology and artificial intelligence for better governance and improved communication with residents.

- Improved disaster risk capacity to deal with climate challenges.

- Biodiversity protections, and increasing annual tree planting rates to plant 100,000 trees by 2050. This is in line with the city's Urban Forest Policy.

- A 120% increase in the city's gross economic output by 2050.

## Transport

### Air



[Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport)'s terminal building, with [Airlink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airlink) planes in front

[Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport) serves both domestic and international flights.[^cite_note-335] It is the second-largest airport in South Africa and serves as a major gateway for travellers to the Cape region. Cape Town has regularly scheduled services to other parts of [Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Africa), [East Africa](260987.md), [Mauritius](19201.md), [Middle East](19323.md), [Far East](11672.md), [Europe](9239.md), [Brazil](3383.md) and the [United States](3434750.md) as well as eleven domestic destinations.[^cite_note-336]

As tourism numbers increased in the lead-up to the tournament of the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md), Cape Town International Airport opened a brand new central terminal building that was developed to handle an expected increase in air traffic.[^cite_note-337]

Other renovations include several large new parking garages, a revamped domestic departure terminal, a new Bus Rapid Transit system station and a new double-decker road system. The airport's cargo facilities are also being expanded and several large empty plots are being developed into [office space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office) and hotels.

Cape Town is one of five internationally recognised [Antarctic gateway cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antarctic_gateway_cities) with transportation connections. Since 2021, commercial flights have operated from Cape Town to [Wolf's Fang Runway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfs_Fang_Runway), Antarctica.[^cite_note-338] The Cape Town International Airport was among the winners of the World Travel Awards for being Africa's leading airport.[^cite_note-339] Cape Town International Airport is located 18 km from the [Central Business District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Business_District).[^cite_note-340]

A second airport has been proposed to serve Cape Town. As of 2025, [Cape Winelands Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Winelands_Airport), currently a private airfield, is undergoing planning for redevelopment. It is situated near [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville), in Cape Town's [Northern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town). If approved and completed, it will become the city's first private airport, and aims to focus on domestic travel, increase Cape Town's global competitiveness as a travel destination, and serve as a backup aviation hub for Cape Town International.[^cite_note-Cape_Winelands_Airport_awaits_decision_on_environmental_assessment_to_begin_construction-341]

CWA's redevelopment is financially backed by South Africa's largest [real estate investment trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate_investment_trust) (REIT), [Growthpoint Properties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Growthpoint_Properties), and the chosen construction contractor is the country's largest of its kind, [WBHO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBHO). The airport aims to be operational by early 2028, and accommodate over 5 million passengers annually by 2050.[^cite_note-New_Cape_Winelands_Airport_a_step_closer_to_taking_off-342]

### Sea



The [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town) is a major transport node in [Africa](5334607.md). In addition to moving freight, it also hosts cruise ships, and serves as a major repair site for both ships and oil rigs

Cape Town has a long tradition as a port city, and its role as a re-provisioning stop at the midpoint of the [Cape Route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Route) gained it the nicknames "Tavern of the Seas" and "Tavern of the Indian Ocean".[^cite_note-343] The [Port of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Cape_Town), the city's main port, is in Table Bay directly to the north of the CBD.

The port is a hub for ships in the southern Atlantic: it is located along one of the busiest shipping corridors in the world, and acts as a stopover point for goods en route to or from Latin America and Asia. It is also an entry point into the South African market.[^cite_note-EU_Handbook_2020-344] It is the second-busiest container port in South Africa after [Durban](313650.md). In 2004, it handled 3,161 ships and 9.2 million tonnes of cargo.[^cite_note-345]

[Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town) Harbour on the False Bay coast of the Cape Peninsula is the main operational base of the [South African Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Navy).

Until the 1970s the city was served by the [Union Castle Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Castle_Line) with service to the United Kingdom and St Helena.[^cite_note-346] The [RMS *St Helena*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RMS_St_Helena_\(1989\)) provided passenger and cargo service between Cape Town and [St Helena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Helena) until the opening of [St Helena Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Helena_Airport).[^cite_note-347]

The cargo vessel M/V *Helena*, under AW Shipping Management, takes a limited number of passengers,[^cite_note-Cargoships-348] between Cape Town and St Helena and [Ascension Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ascension_Island) on its voyages.[^cite_note-349] Multiple vessels also take passengers to and from [Tristan da Cunha](31361.md), inaccessible by aircraft, to and from Cape Town.[^cite_note-350] In addition, NSB Niederelbe Schiffahrtsgesellschaft takes passengers on its cargo service to the [Canary Islands](5717.md) and [Hamburg](13467.md), [Germany](11867.md).[^cite_note-Cargoships-348]

### Rail



[Metrorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrorail_\(Western_Cape\)) train near [Kalk Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalk_Bay) station

The [Shosholoza Meyl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shosholoza_Meyl) is the passenger rail operations of the [Passenger Rail Agency of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passenger_Rail_Agency_of_South_Africa) and operates one long-distance passenger rail service from Cape Town as of 2024: a weekly service to and from [Johannesburg](58893.md) via [Kimberley](313628.md). These trains terminate at [Cape Town railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_railway_station) and make a stop at [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville_railway_station). Cape Town is the terminus for two luxury tourist train routes as of 2024 operated by the Ceres Rail Company, traveling from the [Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V\&A_Waterfront) to [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town) and [Grabouw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grabouw) respectively.

[Metrorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrorail_\(Western_Cape\)) operates a [commuter rail](6286.md) service in Cape Town and the surrounding area. The Metrorail network consists of 96 stations throughout the suburbs and outskirts of Cape Town.

### Road

Cape Town is the origin of three [national routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_routes_in_South_Africa) ("N" routes), namely the N1, N2, and N7.

The [N1](1211545.md) begins at the foreshore area near the Cape Town city centre and runs east-north-east as a freeway through the towns of [Goodwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodwood,_Western_Cape), [Parow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parow,_South_Africa), [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Cape_Town), [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell,_Cape_Town) and [Kraaifontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraaifontein) before continuing towards [Paarl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paarl). It connects Cape Town to major cities further inland, namely [Bloemfontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloemfontein), [Johannesburg](58893.md), and [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria).[^cite_note-\:02-351] An older at-grade road, the [R101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R101_road_\(South_Africa\)), runs parallel to the N1 from Bellville to [Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester,_South_Africa).[^cite_note-\:02-351]

The [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_\(South_Africa\)) begins at the foreshore area near the Cape Town city centre and runs east-south-east through [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town), [Khayelitsha](2766632.md), [Macassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macassar,_Western_Cape) and [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West) before leaving the municipality as the [Sir Lowry's Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Lowry's_Pass), connecting with coastal cities to the east, including [Mossel Bay](370621.md), [George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George,_South_Africa), [Port Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Elizabeth), [East London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_London,_South_Africa) and [Durban](313650.md).[^cite_note-\:02-351] An older at-grade road, the [R102](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R102_\(South_Africa\)), runs parallel to the N1 initially, before veering south-east at Bellville, to join the N2 at Somerset West via the towns of [Kuilsrivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuils_River) and [Eersterivier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eerste_River,_Western_Cape).[^cite_note-\:02-351]

The [N7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N7_\(South_Africa\)) originates from the N1 at Wingfield Interchange near [Acacia Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia_Park,_Cape_Town) and [Edgemead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgemead). It heads northwards, initially as a highway, but becomes an at-grade road from the intersection with the [M12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M12_\(Cape_Town\)) onwards. It follows the Atlantic coast northwards towards [Malmesbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malmesbury), [Clanwilliam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clanwilliam,_South_Africa), [Vredendal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vredendal), [Springbok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springbok,_South_Africa) and the border with [Namibia](21292.md).[^cite_note-\:02-351]



The [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_\(South_Africa\)) as it enters [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD)

There are also a number of [provincial routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_routes_\(South_Africa\)) linking Cape Town with surrounding areas. The [R27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R27_road_\(South_Africa\)) originates from the N1 near the Foreshore and runs north parallel to the N7, but nearer to the coast. It passes through the suburbs of [Milnerton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milnerton,_Cape_Town), [Table View](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_View,_Cape_Town) and [Bloubergstrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloubergstrand,_Cape_Town) and links the city to the West Coast, ending at the town of [Velddrif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velddrif).[^cite_note-\:02-351]

The [R44](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R44_road_\(South_Africa\)) enters the east of the metro from the north, from [Stellenbosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch). It connects Stellenbosch to Somerset West, then crosses the N2 to [Strand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strand,_Western_Cape) and [Gordon's Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon's_Bay). It exits the metro heading south hugging the coast, leading to the towns of [Betty's Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betty's_Bay) and [Kleinmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kleinmond).[^cite_note-\:02-351]

There are also a number of [regional routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_routes_\(South_Africa\)) ("R" routes) in the area. The [R300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R300_road_\(South_Africa\)) is an expressway linking the N1 at [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell) to the N2 near [Mitchells Plain](4240684.md) and the [Cape Town International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport).[^cite_note-\:02-351] The [R302](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R302_road_\(South_Africa\)) runs from the R102 in Bellville, heading north across the N1 through [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville) before leaving the metro to reach [Malmesbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malmesbury,_Western_Cape).[^cite_note-\:02-351]

The [R304](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R304_road_\(South_Africa\)) enters the northern limits of the metro from Stellenbosch, running north-north-west before veering west to cross the N7 at [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia,_Western_Cape) to end at [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis,_Western_Cape) at a junction with the [R307](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R307_road_\(South_Africa\)).[^cite_note-\:02-351] This R307 starts north of [Koeberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg) from the R27 and, after meeting the R304, continues north to [Darling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darling,_Western_Cape).[^cite_note-\:02-351] The [R310](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R310_road_\(South_Africa\)) originates from [Muizenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muizenberg) and runs along the coast, to the south of Mitchell's Plain and Khayelitsha, before veering north-east, crossing the N2 west of Macassar, and exiting the metro heading to Stellenbosch.[^cite_note-\:02-351]



Road infrastructure in the suburb of [Dreyersdal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreyersdal)

Cape Town, like most South African cities, uses [Metropolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Routes_in_Cape_Town) or "M" routes for important intra-city routes, a layer below National (N) roads and Regional (R) routes. Each city's M roads are independently numbered. Most are at-grade roads. The [M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M3_\(Cape_Town\)) splits from the N2 and runs to the south along the eastern slopes of Table Mountain, connecting the [CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD) with [Muizenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muizenberg). Except for a section between Rondebosch and Newlands that has at-grade intersections, this route is a highway. The [M5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M5_\(Cape_Town\)) splits from the N1 further east than the M3, and links the Cape Flats to the CBD. It is a highway as far as the interchange with the M68 at Ottery, before continuing as an at-grade road. Cape Town has the worst traffic congestion in [South Africa](17416221.md) as of 2015.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353]

#### Licensing

Vehicles registered in the City of Cape Town metro use [license plates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/License_plate) denoting the subdivision of registration, from a group allocated by the [provincial government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_the_Western_Cape). Due to population spread, most plates use "CA", while newer vehicles in Cape Town use "CAA", which was introduced in April 2019. Other regions include "CY" for [Durbanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durbanville) and surrounds, "CF" for [Kuils River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuils_River) and surrounds, and "CFM" for [Somerset West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_West) and surrounds. As with the rest of the [Western Cape](313646.md) province, and as opposed to the original letter-number plate format, the most recent license plates issued in Cape Town use the format of two letters, two figures, and two letters (in any sequence), followed by "WC" (for Western Cape).[^cite_note-This_is_what_Cape_Town’s_new_number_plates_will_look_like-354]

In terms of [driver's licensing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drivers_license), Cape Town, and the [Western Cape](313646.md) province, are significantly more advanced than the rest of South Africa. In May 2025, the Western Cape launched a digital [learner's license](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learner's_permit) testing system, the goal of which was to replace the traditional pen and paper test, reduce corruption, and deliver faster and more accurate results.[^cite_note-Western_Cape_launches_digital_learner's_license_testing_system-355]

In September 2025, Cape Town launched its drive through driver's license renewal system, with a facility in [Brackenfell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brackenfell). The system allows for rapid renewal (in under 5 minutes), without driver's leaving their cars. Renewals can be sped up further if driver's have received an SMS renewal confirmation, and show it at the drive through. The first facility is [cashless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cashless), and operates Monday through Saturday.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_launches_first_vehicle_licensing_renewal_drive-through_facility-356]

### Buses

City-owned MyCiTi and privately-owned Golden Arrow both operate scheduled, metro-wide bus services. Several other companies run long-distance bus services between Cape Town and other South African cities.

#### Golden Arrow



[Golden Arrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Arrow_Bus_Service) [electric bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus), by BYD, in Kirstenhof, Cape Town

[Golden Arrow Bus Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Arrow_Bus_Services) (GABS) provides commuter bus services throughout a large portion of Cape Town, and operates a fleet of around 1,200 electric and diesel buses on 1,300 routes, with a daily ridership of around 220,000 people.[^cite_note-Home-357]

The network uses an [NFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_field_communication) Gold Card for cashless payments, and offers discounted fares for weekly and monthly passes, as well as for students and pensioners.[^cite_note-Golden_Arrow_Bus_Service_-_Homepage-358]

GABS operates 7 hubs, situated across the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md).[^cite_note-About_Us-359]

Golden Arrow was the first bus operator in South Africa to roll out [electric buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus), via a partnership with BYD. The first fleet members entered operation in 2024, with more launched in 2025, alongside an expanded charging network.[^cite_note-Western_Cape_rolls_out_new_BYD_electric_buses-360][^cite_note-BYD_Pioneers_South_Africa’s_Electric_Public_Transport_with_120-Electric-Bus_Deal-361]

#### MyCiTi

Main article: [MyCiTi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyCiTi)



A MyCiTi [Optare Solo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optare_Solo) bus

The MyCiTi [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit) (BRT) system is extensive, and operated directly by the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md). It forms a major part of the city's overall public transit system.[^cite_note-The_next_big_step_for_MyCiTi_is_Khayelitsha_and_Mitchells_Plain-362]

The system has a fleet of 223 [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_bus) and [diesel](81761.md) buses, of various sizes. They operate between 42 stations, and around 1,000 stops throughout Cape Town.[^cite_note-Integrated_Annual_Report_2023/2024-363]

The service operates on a combination of dedicated [bus lanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_lane) (painted red and featuring dedicated bus traffic signals), and traditional, mixed-use roadways.

Stations feature free Wi-Fi, enclosed waiting areas, and off-grid power. MyCiTi requires cashless fare payment using mobile [tap-to-pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tap-to-pay), or the company's [EMV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EMV)-compliant card, called myconnect.

The MyCiTi BRT system is being rolled out across four phases. As of 2026, Phase 1A, 1B, and the N2 Corridor have been completed, and Phase 2A is underway.[^cite_note-Phase_2A-364] Phase 2A is the largest investment in public transit by any metro in South Africa in history, and features the construction of an electric bus charging depot, as well as South Africa's first elevated traffic circle.[^cite_note-Cape_Town’s_MyCiti_gets_big_boost_amid_expansion_plans-365]

A procurement agreement has been signed with [Volvo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volvo) Bus Southern Africa for electric buses, with initial deliveries scheduled for 2027.[^cite_note-City_of_Cape_Town_to_procure_30_electric_buses_from_Volvo-366]

### Taxis



A fleet of privately owned taxis in [Camps Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camps_Bay)

Cape Town has various kinds of taxis available, including those obtained through [ride-hailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ride-hailing) (app-based) services such as [Bolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolt_\(company\)) and [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber), as well as traditional metered taxies.

The [Western Cape](313646.md) Provincial Regulatory Entity (PRE) issues ride-hailing licenses for Cape Town, which have expiry dates and need to be renewed. They are processed in the order they are received, and the PRE ensures drivers are operating legally. As of mid-2025, the city has 5,494 registered ride-hailing licenses.[^cite_note-Good_news_for_people_who_use_Uber_and_Bolt_in_Cape_Town-367]

Traditional taxis are either [metered taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicab) or [minibus taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minibus_taxi). Metered taxis can be found at transport hubs as well as other tourist establishments, while minibus taxis can be found at taxi ranks, and, commonly, travelling along main streets.[^cite_note-368] Minibus taxis can be hailed from the road.[^cite_note-369]

Cape Town metered taxi cabs mostly operate in CBD and Cape Town International Airport areas. Large companies that operate fleets of cabs can be reached by phone and are cheaper than the single operators that apply for hire from taxi ranks and [Victoria and Alfred Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_and_Alfred_Waterfront).

There are about 1,000 meter taxis in Cape Town. Their rates vary from R8 per kilometre to about R15 per kilometre. The larger taxi companies in Cape Town are Excite Taxis, Cabnet and Intercab and single operators are reachable by cellular phone. The seven seated Toyota Avanza are the most popular with larger Taxi companies. Meter cabs are mostly used by tourists and are safer to use than minibus taxis.

Minibus taxis are the standard form of transport for the majority of the population who cannot afford private vehicles.[^cite_note-370] Although essential, these taxis are often poorly maintained and are frequently not road-worthy. These taxis make frequent unscheduled stops to pick up passengers, which can cause accidents.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] With the high demand for transport by the working class of South Africa, minibus taxis are often filled over their legal passenger allowance. Minibuses are generally owned and operated in fleets.[^cite_note-373]

## Culture



[Groot Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groot_Constantia), an example of Cape Dutch architecture.



[Mostert's Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostert's_Mill) in Cape Town.

Cape Town is noted for its architectural heritage, with the highest density of [Cape Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Dutch_architecture) style buildings in the world. Cape Dutch style, which combines the architectural traditions of the Netherlands, Germany, France and Indonesia, is most visible in [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town), the [old government buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town) in the Central Business District, and along [Long Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Street_\(Cape_Town\)).[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] The only complete windmill in South Africa is [Mostert's Mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mostert's_Mill), [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town). It was built in 1796 and restored in 1935 and again in 1995.

Cape Town was named the [World Design Capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Design_Capital) for 2014 by the [International Council of Societies of Industrial Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Council_of_Societies_of_Industrial_Design).[^cite_note-376] Cape Town is also recognized as a "[Design City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Design_Cities_\(UNESCO\))" by [UNESCO](21786641.md)'s [Creative Cities Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Cities_Network). The [Artscape Theatre Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre) is the largest performing arts venue in Cape Town.[^cite_note-377]

In 2026, Cape Town was named the 5th-best city in the world for arts and culture, by UK's [*Time Out*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Out_\(magazine\)) magazine.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_voted_one_of_the_world's_best_cities_for_culture!-378]



[Cape Town Minstrel Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaapse_Klopse) (2017).



[Holi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holi) festival at the [Grand Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Parade_\(Cape_Town\)).

The annual [Cape Town Minstrel Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coon_Carnival), also known by its Afrikaans name of *Kaapse Klopse*, is a large [minstrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minstrel) festival held annually on 2 January or *"Tweede Nuwe Jaar"* (Second New Year). Competing teams of minstrels parade in brightly coloured costumes, performing [Cape Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Jazz), either carrying colourful umbrellas or playing an array of musical instruments.

The city also encloses the 36 hectare [Kirstenbosch National Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirstenbosch_National_Botanical_Garden) that contains protected natural forest and [fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fynbos) along with a variety of animals and birds. There are over 7,000 species in cultivation at Kirstenbosch, including many rare and threatened species of the [Cape Floristic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region). In 2004 this Region, including Kirstenbosch, was declared a UNESCO [World Heritage Site](44940.md).[^cite_note-379]

[Whale watching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whale_watching) is popular amongst tourists: [southern right whales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_right_whale) and [humpback whales](231728.md) are seen off the coast during the breeding season (August to November) and [Bryde's whales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryde's_whale) and [orca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orca) can be seen any time of the year.[^cite_note-CTwhale-380] The nearby town of [Hermanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermanus) is known for its Whale Festival, but whales can also be seen in False Bay.[^cite_note-CTwhale-380] [Heaviside's dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heaviside's_dolphin) are endemic to the area and can be seen from the coast north of Cape Town; [dusky dolphins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dusky_dolphin) live along the same coast and can occasionally be seen from the ferry to Robben Island.[^cite_note-CTwhale-380]

### Cuisine

See also: [South African cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_cuisine)



A gatsby sandwich, freshly prepared

Food originating from or synonymous with Cape Town includes the savoury sweet spiced meat dish [Bobotie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobotie) that dates from the 17th century. The [Gatsby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatsby_\(sandwich\)), a sandwich filled with [slap chips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slap_chips) and other toppings, was first served in 1976 in the suburb of [Athlone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athlone,_Cape_Town) and is also synonymous with the city.[^cite_note-381] The [koe'sister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koe'sister) is a traditional [Cape Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Malays) pastry described as a cinnamon infused dumpling with a cake-like texture, finished off with a sprinkling of desiccated coconut.[^cite_note-382]

[Malva pudding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malva_pudding) (sometimes known as Cape Malva pudding) is a sticky sweet dessert often served with hot custard is also associated with the city and dates back to the 17th century.[^cite_note-383] A related dessert dish, [Cape Brandy Pudding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Brandy_Pudding), is also associated with the city and surrounding region.[^cite_note-384] Cape Town is also the home of the [South African wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_wine) industry with the first wine produced in the country being bottled in the city; a number of notable wineries still exist in the city including [Groot Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groot_Constantia) and [Klein Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klein_Constantia).

The city has gained international recognition for its [pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pizza) scene. In 2025, then-new, family-owned restaurant NOVO, based in [Mowbray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowbray,_Cape_Town), ranked in publication *Time Out'*&#x73; Top 20 Pizzas globally. It did so for its "The Fellini" creation.[^cite_note-A_Cape_Town_pizza_has_been_named_in_top_19_globally-385] In the 2026 ranking from the same publication, [Woodstock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodstock,_Cape_Town)-based establishment Pizza Connection ranked 11th on the list, for its "The Capetonian" pizza.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_pizzeria_bags_global_top_spot-386]

[Coffee culture in Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_culture_in_Cape_Town) is thriving, with many independent coffeehouses and cafe chains with locations across the city. All four of South Africa's largest cafe chains, [Vida e Caffè](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vida_e_Caff%C3%A8), [Seattle Coffee Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Coffee_Company), [WCafe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woolworths_\(South_Africa\)), and [Bootlegger Coffee Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bootlegger_Coffee_Company), are headquartered in Cape Town. Furthermore, the city is home to 10 roasteries.

Cape Town is considered one of the best cities in the world for coffee,[^cite_note-Cape_Town_brews_success:_Named_one_of_world's_top_cities_for_coffee-387][^cite_note-Cape_Town's_Coffee_Culture-388] and is known as an international coffee capital.[^cite_note-No_Sleep_for_Cape_Town's_Thriving_Coffee_Culture-389][^cite_note-Coffee_culture_flourishes_in_downtown_Cape_Town-390] In 2024, Cape Town was ranked as the world's 9th best city for coffee by US *Food & Wine Magazine*.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_brews_success:_Named_one_of_world's_top_cities_for_coffee-387] Truth Coffee, situated in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), was voted Best Coffee Shop in the World in 2015 and 2016 by the UK's *[Daily Telegraph](23797577.md)*[^cite_note-Coffee_culture_flourishes_in_downtown_Cape_Town-390] and Espresso Lab in [Woodstock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodstock,_Cape_Town) was voted the 11th best coffee shop in the world, and the best in Africa, in 2025.[^cite_note-You_can_visit_one_of_the_world's_best_coffee_shops_right_here_in_Cape_Town-391]

### Media



A news van owned by [Cape Town TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_TV) outside the [Houses of Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town)

Several newspapers, magazines and printing facilities have their offices in the city. [Independent News and Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_News_and_Media) publishes the major English language papers in the city, the *[Cape Argus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Argus)* and the *[Cape Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Times)*. [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers), the largest media conglomerate in South Africa, publishes *[Die Burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Die_Burger)*, the major Afrikaans language paper.[^cite_note-392]

Cape Town has many local community [newspapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newspapers). Some of the largest community newspapers in English are the *Athlone News* from [Athlone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athlone,_Cape_Town), the *[Atlantic Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Sun)*, the *Constantiaberg Bulletin* from [Constantiaberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantiaberg,_Cape_Town), the *City Vision* from [Bellville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellville,_Cape_Town), the *[False Bay Echo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay_Echo)* from False Bay, Bolander (formerly known as the Helderberg Sun) from [Helderberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helderberg,_Cape_Town), the *[Plainsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plainsman_\(South_Africa\))* from Michell's Plain, the *[Sentinel News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentinel_News)* from Hout Bay, the *[Southern Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Mail_\(newspaper\))* from the Southern Peninsula, the *[Southern Suburbs Tatler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs_Tatler)* from the [Southern Suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Suburbs,_Cape_Town), *[Table Talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Talk_\(Cape_Town\))* from Table View and *Tygertalk* from Tygervalley/Durbanville. Afrikaans language community newspapers include the *[Landbou-Burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landbou-Burger)* and the *Tygerburger*. *[Vukani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vukani)*, based in the Cape Flats, is published in [Xhosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhosa_language).[^cite_note-393]

Cape Town is a centre for major broadcast media with several radio stations that only broadcast within the city. [94.5 Kfm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KFM_\(Cape_Town\)) (94.5 [MHz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MHz) FM) and [Good Hope FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Hope_FM) (94–97 MHz [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_broadcasting)) mostly play [pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music). Heart FM (104.9 MHz FM), the former P4 Radio, plays [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) and [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B), while [Fine Music Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_Music_Radio) (101.3 FM) plays [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_classical_music) and jazz, and Magic Music Radio[^cite_note-394] (828 [kHz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilohertz) MW) plays adult contemporary and classic rock from the '80s, '90s and '00s.[^cite_note-395]

[Bush Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bush_Radio_\(South_Africa\)) is a community radio station (89.5 MHz FM). The [Voice of the Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_the_Cape) (95.8 MHz FM) and [Cape Talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Talk) (567 kHz [MW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediumwave)) are the major [talk radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk_radio) in the city.[^cite_note-396] Bokradio (98.9 MHz FM) is an Afrikaans music station.[^cite_note-397] The [University of Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Cape_Town) also runs its own radio station, [UCT Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCT_Radio) (104.5 MHz FM).

The [SABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SABC) has a small presence in the city, with satellite studios located at [Sea Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_Point). [e.tv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E.tv) has a greater presence, with a large complex located at Longkloof Studios in [Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gardens,_Cape_Town). [M-Net](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M-Net) is not well represented with infrastructure within the city. [Cape Town TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_TV) is a local TV station, supported by numerous organisation and focusing mostly on documentaries.

Numerous productions companies and their support industries are located in the city, mostly supporting the production of overseas commercials, model shoots, TV-series and movies.[^cite_note-398] The local media infrastructure remains primarily in Johannesburg.

### Hiking and trails



[Lion's Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion's_Head_\(Cape_Town\)) hike, with [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain) in the background



Smitswinkel Bay, between [Simon's Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon's_Town) and [Cape Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Point)

Cape Town is home to an array of scenic [nature trails](312678.md) and [hiking](147313.md) routes, which are frequented by locals. Various trails offer options for walking, running, hiking, climbing, cycling, and [dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dog)-walking. Trails in public parks are maintained directly by the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md), while many hiking trails are inside areas that are under [SANParks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SANParks)' jurisdiction.

Popular hiking routes include Lion's Head, Kalk Bay Peak (in Silvermine), Elsie's Peak, Constantia Nek, the Pipe Track, Platteklip Gorge, Maclear's Beacon, Devil's Peak, Kasteelspoort, Tranquillity Cracks, Kloof Corner, Newlands Forest, Skeleton Gorge, Orange Kloof, Noordhoek Panorama Circuit, Noordhoek Peak, Chapman's Peak, Lighthouse Keeper's Trail, Farmer's Cliffs Trail, Tygerberg Nature Reserve, and Blaauwberg Nature Reserve.[^cite_note-The_21_Best_Hiking_Trails_in_Cape_Town-399]

### Sport



[Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium) in [Green Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Point,_Cape_Town)



[Newlands Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Cricket_Ground)



[Newlands Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Stadium)

Cape Town's most popular sports by participation are [cricket](25675557.md), [soccer](10568.md), [swimming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_\(sport\)), and [rugby](25405.md).[^cite_note-400] In rugby union, Cape Town is the home of the [Western Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province_\(rugby\)) side, who play at [Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium) and compete in the [Currie Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Currie_Cup).

In addition, Western Province players (along with some from Wellington's [Boland Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boland_Cavaliers)) form the [Stormers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormers) in the [United Rugby Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Rugby_Championship) competition. Cape Town has also been a host city for both the [1995 Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Rugby_World_Cup) and [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md), and annually hosts the Africa leg of the [World Rugby 7s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rugby_Sevens_Series).[^cite_note-401] Cape Town also hosted the [2023 Netball World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Netball_World_Cup).[^cite_note-402]

[Association football](10568.md), which is mostly known as *soccer* in South Africa, is also popular. [Cape Town City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_City_F.C._\(2016\)) played in the top tier [Premiership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Premiership) until their relegation at the end of the [2024–25 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_South_African_Premiership). A second major club, [Cape Town Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Spurs_F.C.), were relegated from the second tier [National First Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_First_Division) at the end of the [2024–25 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024%E2%80%9325_National_First_Division).

Cape Town also hosted several the matches in the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md) including a semi-final.[^cite_note-southafrica.info-403] In preparation for the World Cup, a new stadium ([Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium)) was constructed in Green Point.

In [cricket](25675557.md), the [Cape Cobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Cobras) represent Cape Town at the [Newlands Cricket Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newlands_Cricket_Ground). The team is the result of an amalgamation of the [Western Province Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province_Cricket) and [Boland Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boland_Cricket) teams. They take part in the [Supersport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersport_Series) and [Standard Bank Cup Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Bank_Cup_Series). The Newlands Cricket Ground regularly hosts international matches.

Cape Town has had Olympic aspirations. For example, in 1996, Cape Town was one of the five candidate cities shortlisted by the [IOC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOC) to launch official candidatures to host the [2004 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Summer_Olympics). Although the Games ultimately went to [Athens](1216.md), Cape Town came in third place.

There has been some speculation that Cape Town was seeking the South African Olympic Committee's nomination to be South Africa's bid city for the [2020 Summer Olympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympic_Games).[^cite_note-404] That was quashed when the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md) awarded the 2020 Games to Tokyo.

The city of Cape Town has vast experience in hosting major national and international sports events. The [Cape Town Cycle Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Cycle_Tour) is the world's largest individually timed [road cycling race](1755460.md) – and the first event outside Europe to be included in the [International Cycling Union's Golden Bike series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCI_Golden_Bike). It sees over 35,000 cyclists tackling a 109 km (68 mi) route around Cape Town.

The [Absa Cape Epic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absa_Cape_Epic) is the largest full-service mountain bike stage race in the world. Some notable events hosted by Cape Town have included the [1995 Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Rugby_World_Cup), [2003 ICC Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_ICC_Cricket_World_Cup), and World Championships in various sports such as athletics, fencing, weightlifting, hockey, cycling, canoeing, gymnastics and others.

Cape Town was also a host city to the [2010 FIFA World Cup](168079.md) from 11 June to 11 July 2010, further enhancing its profile as a major events city. It was also one of the host cities of the [2009 Indian Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Indian_Premier_League) cricket tournament.

The Mother City has also played host to the [Africa leg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_Sevens) of the annual [World Rugby 7s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rugby_Sevens_Series) event since 2015; for nine seasons, from 2002 until 2010, the event was staged in [George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George,_South_Africa) in the Western Cape, before moving to [Port Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Elizabeth) for the [2011 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_South_Africa_Sevens), and then to Cape Town in 2015. The event usually takes place in mid-December, and is hosted at the [Cape Town Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_Stadium) in Green Point.[^cite_note-405]

There are several golf courses in Cape Town. The Clovelly Country Club and Metropolitan Golf Club have 18 holes.[^cite_note-406]

### Water-based activities



[Kitesurfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitesurfing) in [Table Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Bay)

The coastline of Cape Town is relatively long, and the varied exposure to weather conditions makes it fairly common for water conditions to be conducive to recreational scuba diving at some part of the city's coast. There is considerable variation in the underwater environment and regional ecology as there are dive sites on reefs and wrecks on both sides of the Cape Peninsula and False Bay, split between two coastal marine ecoregions by the Cape Peninsula, and also variable by depth zone.



Surfing in [Sandy Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandy_Bay,_Cape_Town)

False Bay is open to the south, and the prevailing open ocean swell arrives from the southwest, so the exposure varies considerably around the coastline. The inshore bathymetry near Cape Point is shallow enough for a moderate amount of refraction of long period swell, but deep enough to have less effect on short period swell, and acts as a filter to pass mainly the longer swell components to the Western shores, although they are significantly attenuated.

The eastern shores get more of the open ocean spectrum, and this results in very different swell conditions between the two sides at any given time. The fetch is generally too short for southeasterly winds to produce good surf. There are more than 20 [named breaks in False Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_Bay#Surfing).

The north-wester can have a long fetch and can produce large waves, but they may also be associated with local wind and be very poorly sorted. The Atlantic coast is exposed to the full power of the South-westerly swell produced by the westerly winds of the southern ocean, often a long way away, so the swell has time to separate into similar wavelengths, and there are some world class big wave breaks among the named breaks of the Atlantic shore.[^cite_note-Wavescape_1-407][^cite_note-Wavescape_2-408][^cite_note-Wavescape_3-409]

Further information on the racing sailboat built in Cape Town: [Cape 31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_31)

In the 1950s the city was part of four editions of the famous [Algiers-Cape Town Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers-Cape_Town_Rally), during the pioneer years of trans-Africa rallying and motor exploration. The closing of the road to the newly founded [Zaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaire) caused the city to be dropped from the traject.[^cite_note-410]

Further information on Water-based activities at Zeekoevlei: [Zeekoevlei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeekoevlei)

Cape Town is also home to [Zeekoevlei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeekoevlei), a fresh water lake near the False Bay coast. Its primary use is for sailing and rowing. Zeekoevlei is the home of the *Zeekoevlei Yacht Club*, as well as a hand full of local schools and university rowing clubs, such as [SACS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_College_Schools), [RBHS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondebosch_Boys_High_School), [Bishops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishops_Diocesan_College), and [UCT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCT).

### Libraries

Cape Town is home to 102 public libraries (101 fixed and 1 satellite location), operated directly by the [City of Cape Town](4043753.md). Visitors can borrow items by registering for a library card.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Libraries-411] The city's libraries loan out books, provide access to electronic resources (via free-to-use computers connected to the internet), and facilitate community programs and events.

Located in Parade Street, close to the Cape Town City Hall, in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), [Central Library Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Library_Cape_Town) is a particularly old library. Established in 1954, the library hosts books in sections including children, teens, adult, art, [LGBTQIA+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQIA+), and local history. The building also houses around 100 public access computers.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_Libraries_Tourism-412] The library contains around 200,000 items (including books, magazines, and DVDs), and has around 5,000 weekly visitors.[^cite_note-The_most_borrowed_books_from_five_Cape_Town_libraries-413]

In 2025, the City launched a fully-digital library service, which includes access to e-books and an online catalogue that features modern and classic literature. The new system also allows for online application of library cards, to reduce queuing time at physical libraries.[^cite_note-Cape_Town_libraries_take_a_big_leap_into_the_digital_age-414]

### Museums and theaters



The main entrance to the [Artscape Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artscape_Theatre_Centre) in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD)

Cape Town has a rich history, and several notable cultural attractions. The [District Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Six) museum provides the public with information about the history of the [Cape Colored](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Coloureds) community and the District Six area. The [Iziko South African Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iziko_South_African_Museum), located in [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), houses important African zoologic, palaeontologic, and archaeologic collections. Located on the same site as the Iziko Museum is the South African National Gallery.

Local theaters include the Artscape Theater, Baxter Theater (owned by the University of Cape Town), and the District Six Homecoming Center.

### Car culture



The [Franschhoek Motor Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek_Motor_Museum), just outside Cape Town

Cape Town has a strong [automotive culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Car_dependency), with numerous annual events and digital communities focused on cars. In-person events include Cars & Coffee, Speed Classic Cape Town, the V\&A Waterfront Motorshow, the Classic Car and Bike Show, the Cape Town Motor Show, Things of Beauty, and Cape WheelFest. The city also hosts The Cape 1,000 grand touring rally. The Tannery Car Show takes place in nearby [Wellington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wellington,_South_Africa).

Automotive [museums](37585.md) in and around the city include the [Franschhoek Motor Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek_Motor_Museum) in [Franschhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek), Wijnland Auto Museum in [Kraaifontein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraaifontein),[^cite_note-Wijnland_Auto_Museum-415] and Tannery Cars, based in Old Tannery, in Wellington.[^cite_note-Tannery_Cars-416]

### Other

Within [Cape Town CBD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_CBD), other cultural attractions include the [Houses of Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houses_of_Parliament,_Cape_Town) (the seat of the South African national government), the Planetarium, and the [Company's Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Company's_Garden) (South Africa's oldest park).
